A file of 1 byte to 10 MiB, `document-upload` attempt limit, then its first bytes must be PDF, JPEG or PNG. Uploads through the session to `documents/<patient>/<uuid>.<ext>`, inserts the row (`source` `staff_upload`, `uploaded_by` = the user, shared or not). If the insert fails the file stays in storage. When shared, `notifyPatient` (topic `messages`, notice "A new document", `documentSharedEmail`, WhatsApp) and a line on whether the patient was told |
| | `setDocumentShared` | Same gate. Sets `shared_with_patient` and `shared_at` (or clears both) on a letter, referral or other document of that patient; no row back gives "You cannot change this document." Sharing tells the patient as above; stopping hides it at once |
| | `archiveDocument` | Same gate and kinds. Sets `archived_at` and `shared_with_patient` false ("Document removed."); the file stays |
| `lib/consents/actions.ts` | `setConsent` | Signed in, not suspended, with a patient view (else `/portal`). Zod: patient UUID, a consent type, yes or no. Inserts a `consents` row with `version` = `CONSENT_VERSION` (RLS needs `can_act_for`); nothing is overwritten. "Consent given." or "Consent withdrawn. The clinic team can see this on your record." (Finding 111). No attempt limit |
| `lib/record/actions.ts` | `addAllergy` | Doctor, nurse or PA with a staff profile, not suspended (else `/clinic`). Substance 1 to 120, reaction up to 200, severity Mild, Moderate, Severe or Anaphylaxis (optional). Inserts with `recorded_by` = own staff id; 42501 gives "You cannot change this patient's record." |
| | `removeAllergy` | [2026-11, `feat/small-items`] Doctor, nurse or PA with a staff profile, not suspended (else `/clinic`). Zod: patient and allergy UUIDs. Sets `archived_at` on that unarchived allergy of that patient through the session; no row back (RLS or already archived) gives "You cannot change this patient's record."; else "Allergy removed." and revalidates the record. The button sits by each allergy, behind a confirmation, for the same roles |
| `lib/portal/actions.ts` | `choosePortalPatient(patientId)` | [2026-11, `feat/small-items`] Called from the switcher, not a form. Zod UUID; the id must be among `getPortalPatients()`, else redirect to `/portal` with no change. Sets cookie `portal_patient` (`httpOnly`, `secure` in production, `sameSite` lax, path `/`, 30 days), then redirects to `/portal`. Not signed, and needs none: it only chooses among rows the session can already read |
| | `addDiagnosis`, `setDiagnosisStatus` | Doctor with a staff profile. Label 1 to 160, ICD-10 code (optional, e.g. `E03.9`), a `diagnosis_status`, a date not in the future (London), notes up to 2,000. The status change updates one diagnosis of that patient |
| | `addSafeguardingAlert`, `resolveSafeguardingAlert` | Doctor with a staff profile. Concern 1 to 120, severity low, moderate or high, note 1 to 4,000, review date from London-time today (optional); `added_by` = own staff id. Resolve sets `status` to `resolved`; the alert stays. Both revalidate `/clinic/safeguarding` |

No attempt limit on the record, consent, share or archive actions. None of them sends anything except as stated.

**Prescription server actions** (`lib/prescriptions/actions.ts`) [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`]. All write through the person's own session; the database functions make the final checks, and their messages are passed on only from a fixed list.

| Action | What it does |
|---|---|
| `requestRepeat` | Signed in, not suspended, with a patient view (else `/portal`). Zod: patient UUID; 1 to 10 items (JSON), each one of `medication_id` or `medicine_id` (UUIDs) or `other_name` (1 to 200), with `supply_months` 1 or 2; appointment answer yes or no; optional yes or no answers; weight up to 200, side effects and note up to 2,000. `repeat-request` limit. Calls `request_repeat` (unanswered questions go as null under a type cast). With Stripe configured, creates a Checkout Session and redirects to Stripe, or to `/portal/prescriptions/[id]?payment=failed` if that fails; without Stripe, calls `announceRequest(id, { paid: false })` (uncommitted [2026-11]) and redirects to `?sent=1` |
| `payRepeat` | Plain form action. The same gate and a request UUID; `repeat-request` limit (`?payment=busy`). Reads the request through the session and opens a new Checkout Session only for an unpaid, not withdrawn, `requested` one |
| `cancelRepeat` | Signed in, not suspended (else `/login`). Calls `cancel_repeat_request`. No attempt limit |
| `decideRepeat` | Doctor or PA, not suspended (else `/clinic`; admins removed [2026-11]). Zod: request UUID; approved, declined or sent_to_pharmacy; note up to 2,000. `repeat-request` limit. Calls `decide_repeat_request`, then tells the patient through `notifyPatient` (topic `messages`; no note, no medicine) and says what reached them |
| `addMedication` | Doctor with a staff profile, not suspended (else `/clinic`). Zod: patient UUID, name 1 to 200, dose up to 120, instructions up to 500, prescription or supplement, pharmacy up to 200, start date, supply 1 to 730 days. Inserts into `medications`, with the doctor as `prescriber_id` for a prescription. No attempt limit |
| `changeMedication` | Same gate. Status active, paused or stopped, start date and supply days; updates the row for that patient (renewing overwrites `start_date`). No attempt limit |
| `addListMedicine`, `setListMedicineArchived` | Admin or doctor, not suspended (else `/clinic`). Name 1 to 120 and a weight-loss box; or take off and put back. A duplicate name gives "already on the list". No attempt limit |

**Feedback server actions** (`lib/feedback/actions.ts`) [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`]. Through the person's own session; database messages are passed on only from a fixed list, otherwise a generic message.

| Action | What it does |
|---|---|
| `submitFeedback` | Signed in (else `/login`), not suspended (else `/portal`). Zod: feedback UUID; score 0 to 10; the two comments trimmed, up to 2,000 each; `wantsContact` and `mayQuote` each `yes` or `no`. `feedback` attempt limit. Calls `submit_feedback`. For amber or red, emails `feedbackAlertEmail` (red: "today") to `FEEDBACK_ALERT_TO` or `info@theschoemanclinic.com`, with only a link to `/clinic/feedback/<id>`; a failed email is logged and does not stop the answer. Revalidates `/portal` and `/clinic/feedback`, then redirects to `/portal/feedback/<id>` (the thank-you) |
| `recordFollowUp` | Admin, PA or doctor, not suspended (else `/clinic`). Zod: feedback UUID; note 1 to 2,000 after trimming. `feedback` attempt limit. Calls `record_feedback_follow_up`; returns "Follow-up recorded." |
| `checkQuote` | [2026-11, `2171c59`] Admin, PA or doctor, not suspended (else `/clinic`). Zod: feedback UUID; `checked` `yes` or `no`. `feedback` attempt limit. Calls `check_feedback_quote`; on error passes on "Only comments the patient agreed may be quoted can be checked.", otherwise "You cannot check this feedback."; revalidates `/clinic/feedback` (layout); returns "Checked. These comments are now in the evidence download." or "Check taken back. These comments are no longer in the evidence download." |
**SignatureRx server actions** (`lib/signaturerx/actions.ts`) [2026-11, `signaturerx`]. All three write through the person's own session; the database functions do the final checks.

| Action | What it does |
|---|---|
| `sendToSignatureRx` | Doctor only, not suspended, and `isSignatureRxSigningEnabled()` (else an error message). Zod: request UUID; `draft` or `issueOnly`; 1 to 10 medicines (JSON: description up to 200, quantity up to 60, directions up to 500); an optional integration code (up to 200); a six-digit PIN. `signaturerx` attempt limit. In order: reads the request through the doctor's session (must be `approved` and not withdrawn); calls `can_send_to_signaturerx` through the session (else an error that the doctor must be on the care team and never for themselves); `getPatientForSignatureRx` must find address, phone, email, country, **sex** and date of birth, else the error lists what is missing and tells the doctor to ask a PA to add it on the record; needs an IPv4 address from the request headers; takes the typed integration code or reads the saved one with the service role. Then `start_signaturerx_send` (service role; 23505 gives "already sent, or being sent", 42501 the care-team message) opens a `sending` row, `createPrescription` (`POST /api/v1/ehr-prescription-patient`, `notify: false`, `client_ref_id` the request id) creates it, and `finish_signaturerx_send` closes the row with the id and state. A clear refusal (`auth` or `invalid` kind with an HTTP status) calls `fail_signaturerx_send` and allows another try; a timeout or unreadable reply leaves the send open as `sending` with a message to check SignatureRx; if closing the row fails the message names the SignatureRx id and says not to send again. **A typed integration code is saved (`set_my_signaturerx_code`, doctor's session) only after SignatureRx accepts the send.** The PIN is never stored, logged or returned. Revalidates `/clinic/prescriptions/[id]` |
| `checkSignatureRxStatus` | Doctor or PA, not suspended. Zod: row UUID. `signaturerx` attempt limit. Reads the row through the session; a row with no SignatureRx id (`sending` or `failed`) is not asked about; a final status (`dispensed`, `voided`, `expired`, `collected`) is not asked about again. Asks `GET /api/v1/prescriptions/{id}` with the sender's integration code (service role read), then `refresh_signaturerx_prescription` |
| `resolveSignatureRxSend` | [2026-11, third pass] Doctor or admin, not suspended (else "Only an admin or a doctor can settle this."). Zod: row UUID, request UUID, mode `confirm` or `close`, and one trimmed value of 1 to 200 characters (the SignatureRx id, or what was found). `signaturerx` attempt limit. Calls `confirm_signaturerx_send` or `close_signaturerx_send` through the person's session; the database checks the care team and the row. 42501 gives the care-team message, 23505 "already recorded for another request", 22023 "no longer waiting for a check, or the box is empty". Revalidates the prescription page. Success text: after a confirm, "Use Check SignatureRx to bring in its state"; after a close, "The request can be sent again." |

`lib/signaturerx/client.ts` (`server-only`): `accessToken` (POST `/oauth/token`, client credentials, cached in memory until five minutes before expiry; cleared on 401), `call` (Bearer token, optional `integration-code` header, 15 and 20 second timeouts, `cache: "no-store"`, **`redirect: "error"`** so a redirect never resends a PIN, code or patient details elsewhere; 400, 401, 403 and 422 become a readable `SignatureRxError` with no secrets in it), `checkConnection`, `createPrescription`, `getPrescription`. `lib/signaturerx/constants.ts`: status labels, `SRX_CLOSED_STATUSES` (`failed`, `voided`, `expired`), final statuses, the two actions, `srxStatusLabel` (`sending` reads "Not confirmed: check SignatureRx", `failed` "Not sent").

**Server actions** (`lib/auth/actions.ts`): `signIn` (email and password, Zod-checked, attempt limits first, one message for every failure, signs out and shows "no access" if the account has no area, sets `ch_session_kind` and `ch_last_seen`), `requestPasswordReset` (attempt limits first; the screen always says "sent", whether or not the account exists, and adds "If nothing arrives in a few minutes, try again later or contact the clinic."; a Supabase 429 is logged on the server, not shown; the email link goes to `/auth/confirm?next=/update-password`), `updatePassword` (12+ characters, lower, upper, digit; sets `ch_session_kind` and `ch_last_seen`), `signOut` (also clears both `ch_` cookies). `signIn` also reads a hidden `next` field: after the usual checks it redirects to it only if `parseReturnPath` accepts it and the person is staff, otherwise to their home. `/login?next=...` does the same for an already signed-in person; the login form passes `returnPath` as the hidden field.

**Profile server actions** (`lib/profile/actions.ts`) [2026-11, uncommitted]. Every action starts with `requireUser()` (no session or a suspended profile goes to `/login`) and takes the person from the session: no form carries an id. All read and write through the user's own session; none uses the service role except `allowAuthAttempt`.

| Action | What it does |
|---|---|
| `updateDetails` | Zod: name 1 to 120 characters, phone up to 30 of digits, spaces, `+`, `(`, `)`, `-`; updates `profiles.full_name` and `phone` for the person's own row |
| `uploadAvatar`, `removeAvatar` | Photo: declared type jpeg, png or webp, at most 2 MiB, first bytes must match the type; uploads with `upsert` to `avatars/<user id>/avatar`, then sets `profiles.avatar_path`. Remove sets `avatar_path` to null; the file stays |
| `uploadSignature` | Doctors only (`roles` includes `doctor`); same checks at 1 MiB; uploads to `signatures/<user id>/signature`, then sets `staff_profiles.signature_path`; revalidates `/clinic/profile` |
| `changePassword` | Checks new password rules and the match, then the `password-change` limit, then re-checks the current password with `signInWithPassword`, then `updateUser({ password })`, then `signOut({ scope: "others" })`. Supabase's own "password changed" email follows |
| `requestEmailChange` | Same checks and the `email-change` limit, then `updateUser({ email })`. The reply is the same whether or not the address is taken; Auth sends the confirmation emails (double confirm is set; one link was enough locally, `docs/SECURITY.md` S-68). The trigger updates `profiles.email` when confirmed |
| `signOutOtherDevices` | `signOut({ scope: "others" })` |
| `saveNotificationPreferences` | Reads the person's rows; writes only rows that differ, and only `enabled` on an existing row; a missing row counts as on, so only an "off" inserts one. Topics: results, payments, appointments, messages. Channels: email, portal. [2026-11, `feat/clinic-build-2`] With GoHighLevel configured the channels are email, WhatsApp and portal; a missing row counts as the channel's default (WhatsApp off), so only a change from the default inserts a row |

Most call `revalidatePath("/", "layout")`, so the name and photo in the top bar refresh. Pages are rendered per request, so this saves nothing and is harmless.

**Data flow of photos and signatures** [2026-11, uncommitted]. The browser posts the form to the server action (there is no browser Supabase client). The action checks the file, then uploads through the person's own session to the private bucket at the one fixed path, so the Storage policies decide again. A page that shows an image asks Storage for a signed URL (3600 seconds) through the viewer's own session, so Storage decides who gets one: the person's own photo, any active staff member's, a patient's photo for the team; a signature only for its owner. [2026-11, `20261113120100`] Now: a patient or guardian gets only their own photo; staff get team photos and the photos of patients they can open. The URL goes into the page and the browser loads it from the Supabase origin, which the CSP's `img-src` allows (`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`). The top-bar photo comes from the layout (`getAvatarUrl(user.avatarPath)`); the profile page shows its own copy. The doctor's signature is shown only on her Signature tab; letters will read it with the service role in server code when they are built.

**Invitation server actions** (`lib/invitations/actions.ts`, `accept-actions.ts`) [2026-11, uncommitted]. They use the service-role client (`createAdminClient`) after checking who is asking in code; the database functions check the named person again. No form field carries an inviter, a role for a patient, or a hash: the inviter is `getCurrentUser()`, and the kind, role, patient and address of an acceptance come from the invitation row, never from the form.

| Action | What it does |
|---|---|
| `sendInvitation` | (For a patient or guardian invitation, an existing patient or new minimal details, `newPatientSchema`: first and last name, date of birth. Sends `_confirm_email_mismatch` from the `confirmMismatch` checkbox; on the `email_mismatch` hint it returns `needsConfirm` so the form asks the sender to confirm.) `requireInviter` (a PA or admin, not suspended; anyone else goes to `/clinic`), Zod by kind (patient, guardian, team), `kindsFor(roles)`, the `invitation-send` limit, a fresh token, `create_invitation`, then the email through `lib/email/send.ts` with the link `${SITE_URL}/invite?token=…`. If the email variables are missing or Resend fails, the invitation is saved and the form says so ("use Resend"). [2026-11] `requireInviter` is now "`kindsFor(roles)` is not empty", so doctors pass; the database limits them to their own patients. After a **team** invitation for a doctor or an admin, `alertTeamInvite` emails `teamInviteAlertEmail` to `TEAM_INVITE_ALERT_TO` (`drgina@theschoemanclinic.com`); a failure or missing email settings is logged and never blocks or reports to the sender. The alert is sent whether or not the invitation email itself went out, and not on a resend. [2026-11] Since `8399375` the alert is sent only when `VERCEL_ENV` is `production`; since `2847f26` it goes to `process.env.TEAM_INVITE_ALERT_TO`, else `TEAM_INVITE_ALERT_TO_DEFAULT` (`drgina@theschoemanclinic.com`) |
| `resendInvitation` | The same checks; reads the invitation through the person's session first (a PA cannot see a team invitation, so it is "not found"), then `resend_invitation` and a new email |
| `cancelInvitation` | `revoke_invitation`. No attempt limit |
| `createAccountFromInvitation` | Reads the invitation from the cookie; refused unless valid and no account exists. Zod: name 1 to 120, the shared password rules, repeat. `invitation-accept` limit. `auth.admin.createUser` with `email_confirm: true` (the link reaching the address is the proof), `checkIdentity` (patient and guardian: the `dob` field through `verify_invitation_identity`; "wrong" and "locked" give their own messages), then `accept_invitation` with `_date_of_birth`, then `signInWithPassword`, `startSessionCookies` (kind `staff` for a team invitation, otherwise `patient`), clears `ch_invite`, and redirects to `/clinic` for a team invitation or `/portal`. `create_invitation` allows one live patient invitation per record, and `get_invitation` reads as `cancelled` for an archived or already-registered patient, so the usual refusals come before the account is made. If acceptance is still refused late, the account stays with status `invited` (`docs/SECURITY.md` S-77, reduced) |
| `signInToAcceptInvitation` | For an invitation whose address has an account: the `sign-in` limit for the address and the `invitation-accept` limit for the link, `signInWithPassword`, session cookies, back to `/invite/accept`. It refuses a suspended account and signs it out (S-78, resolved) |
| `acceptInvitationWhileSignedIn` | The signed-in, not-suspended person; the same `checkIdentity` date of birth; the invitation must be valid and its address equal the person's email (also checked in the database); `invitation-accept` limit; `accept_invitation`; session cookies; clears `ch_invite`; redirects |
| `signOutForInvitation` | Signs the person out and returns to `/invite/accept` |

**Attempt limits** (`lib/auth/throttle.ts`, `allowAuthAttempt`) [2026-11, uncommitted]. Sign-in and reset reach Supabase from this server, so Supabase's own limits cannot tell one visitor from another.

| Action | Window | By address | By address and account | By account |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sign-in | 15 minutes | 60 | 10 | 30 |
| Password reset | 1 hour | 20 | 3 | 3 |
| Password change [2026-11, uncommitted] | 15 minutes | 60 | 8 | 20 |
| Email change [2026-11, uncommitted] | 1 hour | 20 | 5 | 5 |
| Invitation send (`invitation-send`; keyed by the inviter's email; send and resend) [2026-11, uncommitted] | 1 hour | 80 | 40 | 40 |
| Results entry (`results-entry`; keyed by the staff member's email) [2026-11, uncommitted] | 1 hour | 300 | 120 | 120 |
| Invitation accept (`invitation-accept`; keyed by the first 40 characters of the link's hash) [2026-11, uncommitted] | 15 minutes | 60 | 10 | 15 |
| Booking (`booking`; keyed by the person's email) [2026-11, `29f602f`] | 1 hour | 60 | 20 | 20 |
| Questions (`question`; new questions and replies from either side, keyed by the person's email) [2026-11, `feat/finalise-points`] | 1 hour | 120 | 30 | 30 |
| Document upload (`document-upload`; keyed by the staff member's email) [2026-11, `feat/finalise-points`] | 1 hour | 300 | 120 | 120 |
| Repeat requests (`repeat-request`; keyed by the person's email; `requestRepeat`, `payRepeat`, `decideRepeat`) [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`] | 1 hour | 200 | 40 | 40 |
| Feedback (`feedback`; keyed by the person's email; `submitFeedback`, `recordFollowUp` and, since `2171c59`, `checkQuote` share it) [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] | 1 hour | 200 | 30 | 30 |
| Online booking (`online-booking`; keyed by the email typed on the public form, so the "account" is that address, not a signed-in person; `bookOnline` only) [2026-11, `4f3c29c`] | 1 hour | 15 | 5 | 5 |
| SignatureRx (`signaturerx`; keyed by the person's email; `sendToSignatureRx`, `checkSignatureRxStatus`) [2026-11, `signaturerx`] | 1 hour | 60 | 20 | 20 |

A refused attempt gets "Too many attempts. Please wait a few minutes and try again.", which says nothing about the account, because the app's own limit treats every address alike. A Supabase 429 on sign-in gets the same message. A Supabase 429 on reset gets the normal "sent" reply and is logged without the address, since Supabase's email limits apply only to real accounts. The address is `x-real-ip`, then the first `x-forwarded-for`; with neither, only the account bucket applies. If the check itself fails, the attempt goes ahead and an error is logged with no email or address: an outage here must not stop people signing in.

**Routing** (`lib/auth/session.ts`): `getCurrentUser` is cached per request and reads the profile, roles and `get_my_patient_record()`. `homePathFor`: suspended gives no area; any staff role gives `/clinic`; otherwise anyone with a patient record (patient or verified guardian) gives `/portal`. The `/portal` and `/clinic` layouts redirect anyone else. A person who is both (the seed doctor-patient) gets a switch link in the shell. These checks only route; row-level security decides what is readable.

**Patient home** (`getPortalHome`) [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`: when the portal person can act and the patient has an open survey (`getOpenSurvey`), an info alert bar "How was the appointment?" links to it, with a "Give feedback" button in the working tree] [2026-11, `6164a90`: the next-appointment, results and latest-letter cards now link to `/portal/appointments`, `/portal/results` and `/portal/documents`]: bloods alert bar when an open order has a due date (as briefed), next appointment, bloods card (as in the prototype), new results card from the newest released report, up to four markers at a glance with sparklines (out of range first), latest shared letter with its author named through `get_staff_directory()`, and a running-low medication notice (14 days). Each query also filters to the patient and to released data itself, because a doctor who is also a patient is a clinical reader too.

**Clinic dashboard** (`getClinicHome`): today's count and next appointment, reports to review (and how many have an out-of-range value), imports waiting, an attention list (the marker that matters most per unreviewed report, with the previous released value), the diary for the day filtered by location, and "Bloods at risk" cards for appointments in the next 14 days with an open order. The location is chosen in the URL (`?location=`) by the top-bar switcher [2026-11], not by the prototype's chips. An admin sees no patient data (RLS); a health coach sees no unreleased results. [2026-11] Out of date since `20261112120100`: an admin sees every patient's data here, and doctors, nurses and health coaches only their care team's, unreleased results included (no code change in `lib/data/clinic.ts`). [2026-11, `0539a05`] The code comments now say the same (Finding 73).

**API routes**: `/auth/confirm`, `/invite`, and `/api/google/connect` and `/api/google/callback` [2026-11], and `/api/transcription/webhook` [2026-11, `feat/clinic-build-2`], and the GET route handlers `/portal/documents/[id]` and `/clinic/documents/[id]` [2026-11, `feat/finalise-points`], and `/api/cron/revoke-google` [2026-11, `feat/small-items`], and `/api/stripe/webhook` and `/api/cron/prescription-reminders` [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`], the latter replaced by `/api/cron/patient-messages` [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`], and the GET route handler `/clinic/feedback/export` [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`]. Not built: Stripe for lab orders, lab and sync webhooks, and any inbound GoHighLevel webhook.

**Cookies for invitations**: `ch_invite` holds the link's token between the email and the accept page (see `/invite`). It and `ch_google_state` (`Path=/api/google`, see `/api/google/connect`) [2026-11] are the only cookies with a path other than `/`. It is cleared when an invitation is accepted, not when an attempt fails; it expires after an hour.

## 7. Environment variables

From `.env.example` and `lib/env.ts`. `.env*.local` is ignored.

| Variable | Scope | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` | Public | Supabase project URL (local stack: `http://127.0.0.1:54321`) |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` | Public | Publishable (anon) key |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEMO` | Public, optional | `true` shows the demo banner |
| `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` | Server only | Used only from `lib/supabase/admin.ts`. Sign-in and reset use it for the attempt limits (without it they fail open). **Invitations [2026-11] cannot be sent, cancelled, looked up or accepted without it**, and the staff Calendar cannot save, read or remove a Google token without it [2026-11]. Since `feat/clinic-build-2` [2026-11] it is also needed for every patient message (`notifyPatient`), the booking calendar event, and sending and collecting transcripts. Since `feat/finalise-points` [2026-11] also for the portal notices `notifyPatient` writes and for telling a patient about a shared document [2026-11, `4f3c29c`] **The public booking pages `/book` and `/book/details` cannot render without it** (`getSupabaseServerEnv` throws on the first read), and `bookOnline` cannot book. [2026-11, `49cb8b8`] Two optional variables were added for the bot check (rows below); the confirmation email also needs `SITE_URL` and the Resend variables, or GoHighLevel for WhatsApp |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY`, `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` | Site key public (read directly from `process.env` by `/book/details`); secret server only (`getTurnstileSecret()` in `lib/env.ts`, accepted only at 20 to 200 characters, else treated as unset) | [2026-11, `49cb8b8`]. Optional. Cloudflare Turnstile on the public booking form. Both are needed: with only the site key the widget shows but nothing is verified; with only the secret every booking is refused, because no widget produces a token (Finding 176). Commented in `.env.example` |
| `GOOGLE_WORKSPACE_DOMAIN` | Server only, optional | Only Google accounts in this Workspace domain can connect a calendar: sent as the `hd` hint and checked against the account's `hd` after sign-in (`lib/calendar/google.ts`). Validated in `lib/env.ts` (`getGoogleEnv`). In `.env.example`; this table did not list it until the merge |
| `SESSION_COOKIE_SECRET` | Server only | Signs the `ch_last_seen` and `ch_session_kind` cookies; 32 characters or more (`openssl rand -base64 48`). Read by `getSessionEnv` in `lib/env.ts`. Required: the proxy fails closed without it. Set it in Vercel and `.env.local`; `web-local` sets a fixed local value. Changing it signs everyone out |
| `RESEND_API_KEY` | Server only | Used only from `lib/email/send.ts` and `pnpm email:test`. Invitations and result release call the wrapper [2026-11]; without the key (or `EMAIL_FROM` or `SITE_URL`) an invitation is saved and the screen says email is not set up. Restrict the key to sending on the one domain |
| `EMAIL_FROM` | Server only | Sender address on the domain verified in Resend |
| `SITE_URL` | Server only | Required with the email variables (`z.url`, no trailing slash). Links and the logo in emails are built from it, never from a request. Set it per environment [2026-11]. Also required by the Google Calendar: the OAuth redirect URI is built from it (a trailing slash is stripped there) [2026-11]. Also required by transcription (the webhook URL given to AssemblyAI) and read through `getEmailEnv()` by booking and result messages [2026-11]. Since `2847f26` [2026-11] patient messages (booking, results, question replies, shared documents) read it through `getSiteUrl()`, which needs `SITE_URL` alone |
| `EMAIL_REPLY_TO` | Server only, optional | Where replies go; `send.ts` falls back to it. Open question: which address (PRD) |
| `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`, `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` | Server only | Google OAuth client (Web application) for the staff Calendar [2026-11]. Read by `getGoogleEnv` and `isGoogleConfigured` in `lib/env.ts` (non-empty strings). Register `<SITE_URL>/api/google/callback` as the redirect URI in Google Cloud Console. If any Google variable or `SITE_URL` is missing or invalid, `/clinic/calendar` says it is not set up and `/api/google/connect` redirects there with `?error=not-set-up` |
| `GOOGLE_TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY` | Server only | Must decode from base64 to exactly 32 bytes (`openssl rand -base64 32`), checked by `lib/env.ts`. Encrypts each staff member's Google refresh token (AES-256-GCM, user id as additional data, `lib/calendar/crypto.ts`) before it is stored. Changing it makes every stored token unreadable: each person's connection is deleted the next time it is used and they are asked to connect again |
| `GHL_PRIVATE_TOKEN`, `GHL_LOCATION_ID` | Server only, optional | [2026-11, `feat/clinic-build-2`] GoHighLevel Private Integration token (scopes for contacts write and conversation-message write) and the clinic's sub-account (location) id, read by `getGhlEnv` and `isGhlConfigured` (non-empty). Both set: patient messages go through GoHighLevel and WhatsApp is offered in notification choices. Either missing: email goes through Resend and WhatsApp is neither offered nor sent. Commented out in `.env.example` |
| `ASSEMBLYAI_API_KEY` | Server only, optional | [2026-11] AssemblyAI key (EU region) for call transcription, read by `getTranscriptionEnv` and `isTranscriptionConfigured`. Without it, or without the secret below or `SITE_URL`, the recorder is hidden, recording actions refuse and the webhook answers 404. Needs a data processing agreement with AssemblyAI (comment in `lib/env.ts`) |
| `TRANSCRIPTION_WEBHOOK_SECRET` | Server only, optional | [2026-11] At least 32 characters (`openssl rand -base64 48`). Sent to AssemblyAI with each job and expected back in the `x-clinic-hub-secret` header on `/api/transcription/webhook`. Changing it makes jobs already submitted answer with the old secret (401); "Check now" still collects them |
| `CRON_SECRET` | Server only, optional | [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] Gates `/api/cron/patient-messages` (which replaces `/api/cron/prescription-reminders`); without it no run-out reminders and no feedback surveys are sent. [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`] Also gates `/api/cron/prescription-reminders`; without it no run-out reminders are sent. [2026-11, `feat/small-items`] At least 32 characters (`openssl rand -base64 48`). Vercel Cron sends it as a bearer token to `/api/cron/revoke-google`; without it, or if shorter, the route answers 404 and leavers' tokens stay queued. Read from `process.env` in the route, not through `lib/env.ts` |
| `TEAM_INVITE_ALERT_TO` | Server only, optional | [2026-11, `2847f26`] Who is emailed when a doctor or admin is invited. Read from `process.env` in `lib/invitations/actions.ts`, not validated; empty or unset means `drgina@theschoemanclinic.com`. Commented out in `.env.example`. The alert is only sent on Vercel production (`VERCEL_ENV`) and needs the Resend variables (Finding 76 resolved) |
| `QUESTIONS_ALERT_TO` | Server only, optional | [2026-11, `feat/finalise-points`] Who is emailed, with no details, when a patient asks a question. Read from `process.env` in `lib/questions/actions.ts`, not validated; empty or unset means `info@theschoemanclinic.com`. Sent through Resend, so it needs `RESEND_API_KEY`, `EMAIL_FROM` and `SITE_URL`; **not** limited to production (Finding 106). Commented out in `.env.example` |
| `LAB_WEBHOOK_SECRETS` | Server only, optional | [2026-11, `feat/lab-webhook`, uncommitted] One secret per lab provider as `name:secret` pairs, separated by commas or new lines; the secret at least 32 characters (`openssl rand -base64 48`). The name is the last part of the webhook address (`<SITE_URL>/api/webhooks/lab-results/<name>`); the lab sends the secret as `Authorization: Bearer <secret>`. Parsed by `getLabWebhookSecrets` in `lib/env.ts`. Unset means the webhook answers 404 for every provider. In `.env.example`, commented out. Not recorded as set on any environment |
| `LAB_ALERT_TO` | Server only, optional | [2026-11, `feat/lab-webhook`, uncommitted] Who is emailed, with no details, when a lab file arrives. Read from `process.env` in `lib/labs/notify.ts`, not validated; empty or unset means `info@theschoemanclinic.com`. Sent through Resend |
| `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` | Server only, optional | [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`] Stripe secret or restricted key (`sk_` or `rk_`, then `test_` or `live_`), checked by `getStripeEnv` and `isStripeConfigured` with the next variable and `SITE_URL`. Without all three, repeat requests are sent unpaid and `/api/stripe/webhook` answers 404 |
| `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` | Server only, optional | [2026-11] The signing secret (`whsec_`) of the endpoint `<SITE_URL>/api/stripe/webhook`, subscribed to `checkout.session.completed` and `checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded` |
| `PRESCRIPTIONS_ALERT_TO` | Server only, optional | [2026-11] Who is emailed, with no details, when a repeat is paid for. Read from `process.env` in `lib/prescriptions/notify.ts`, not validated; empty or unset means `info@theschoemanclinic.com` |
| `FEEDBACK_ALERT_TO` | Server only, optional | [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] Who is emailed, with no details, when feedback needs a follow-up (amber or red). Read from `process.env` in `lib/feedback/actions.ts`, not validated; empty or unset means `info@theschoemanclinic.com`. Needs the Resend variables and `SITE_URL`; not limited to production. Commented out in `.env.example` |
| `GOOGLE_REVIEW_URL` | Server only, optional | [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] The clinic's Google review link (Google Business Profile), offered on the thank-you page after a green answer only ([2026-11, `38a918f`]; until then to everyone who answered). Read by `getGoogleReviewUrl()` in `lib/env.ts`: an `https` URL or nothing (the link is hidden). Commented out in `.env.example` |
| `SIGNATURERX_CLIENT_ID` | Server only, optional | [2026-11, `signaturerx`] The OAuth client id of the clinic's SignatureRx account (client credentials). Checked by `getSignatureRxEnv` and `isSignatureRxConfigured` together with the next two. Without all three the SignatureRx section is hidden or off. Password manager first, then Vercel only when the clinic is ready; never committed |
| `SIGNATURERX_CLIENT_SECRET` | Server only, optional | [2026-11] The matching secret. Never printed by `pnpm srx:check` |
| `SIGNATURERX_BASE_URL` | Server only, optional | [2026-11] Exactly `https://stage-srx.signaturerx.co.uk` (testing) or `https://app.signaturerx.co.uk` (live); any other value fails validation |
| `SIGNATURERX_SIGNING_ENABLED` | Server only, optional | [2026-11] Must be exactly `true` (and the three above set) for `isSignatureRxSigningEnabled`, which shows the PIN form and lets `sendToSignatureRx` run. Read from `process.env`. Off by default |
| `VERCEL_ENV` | Set by Vercel | [2026-11, `8399375`] Read by `alertTeamInvite`: the doctor or admin invitation alert is sent only when it is `production`. Not in `.env.example` or `lib/env.ts` |

**Environments** [2026-11]. One hosted Supabase project for now, with the local stack as development. `.env.local` points at the hosted project, so `pnpm dev` and the `web` configuration use hosted data. Use `web-local` (port 3130) for the local stack. Agents never connect to the hosted project; a human links and pushes. Password reset on the hosted project is unproven until SMTP is set up.

## 8. Technical decisions

- [2026-11] Tailwind v4 rather than the template's v3, to match the brief.
- [2026-11] Default Tailwind palette, radii and shadows removed with `--color-*: initial` (and equivalents), so stray utilities generate nothing and only tokens exist.
- [2026-11] Utilities are named after tokens. shadcn's `--secondary` role is kept as `--secondary-role`; `secondary` is the bronze token.
- [2026-11] `cacheComponents: true` removed: all portal data is per-user and must not be cached.
- [2026-11] Colima is the local Docker runtime on this machine (Docker was not installed). `pnpm db:start` needs it running.
- [2026-11] Screens read through the signed-in user's session; the service role lives only in `lib/supabase/admin.ts`.
- [2026-11] No cascade deletes; clinical records archived with `archived_at`; accounts suspended through `profiles.status`.
- [2026-11] No remote Supabase project is linked or pushed to by agents.
- [2026-11] Row-level security does the authorisation; the server layouts and `proxy.ts` only route. All screens read through the signed-in session.
- [2026-11] Invitations, role grants, result release and import confirmation are server code only, so a client can never choose a token, a role or a release.
- [2026-11] Read functions use a private definer body and a public invoker wrapper, because the advisors warn on definer functions in an exposed schema.
- [2026-11] One permissive policy per command per table, combining roles with OR, to keep the `multiple_permissive_policies` advisor quiet.
- [2026-11] Audit stores changed column names only, never values; the log cannot be erased.
- [2026-11] `pg_graphql` dropped so table names are not discoverable through GraphQL.
- [2026-11] Clinic location is selected in the URL (`?location=`) by the top-bar switcher, so the server page reads it.
- [2026-11] The patient home shows the bloods alert bar as briefed as well as the prototype's bloods card.
- [2026-11] `supabase migration new` hangs without a terminal; use `</dev/null`.
- [2026-11] Profile: patient contact details on the record are read-only in the app ("contact the clinic"); notification channels are email and portal only until GoHighLevel is connected; the sign-in email can be changed by anyone, double-confirmed; signatures are for doctors and live in their own private bucket; photos are for everyone. See the PRD.
- [2026-11] Password and email changes re-check the current password in the app with `signInWithPassword`. That issues a new Auth session and restarts the staff 12-hour total; the Auth API itself does not ask for the password (`docs/SECURITY.md` S-37, S-68, S-72).
- [2026-11] The shell's current nav row is read from the pathname in a Client Component, so the same sidebar works on the profile route.
- [2026-11] The default branch is `main`.
- [2026-11] One hosted Supabase environment for now, with the local stack as development, decided by the owner.
- [2026-11] Email templates are built (section 5) in the design system's look with hex colours, inline styles and system-font fallbacks, because email clients lack CSS variables, oklch and web fonts. Resend, custom SMTP and the hosted Auth templates are still **manual and not done**, waiting on the domain being verified.
- [2026-11] Email links come from `SITE_URL`, not a request header, and no email carries a clinical value.
- [2026-11] Leaked password protection is off, because it needs the Pro plan.
- [2026-11] No browser Supabase client, so the auth cookies can be `HttpOnly`. A Client Component that needs data must go through the server.
- [2026-11] Session limits live in the proxy, because Supabase's time-box and inactivity settings need the Pro plan. It applies only to requests through the proxy, so it cannot stop a token copied out of the browser and used directly against Supabase.
- [2026-11] Attempt limits live in Postgres behind the service role, so they hold across server instances. They fail open, because an outage must not stop people signing in. The address limit is well above the account limits because a clinic shares one address.
- [2026-11] The Content-Security-Policy is built per request in the proxy with a nonce and `strict-dynamic`, so injected script cannot run; styles keep `'unsafe-inline'`. It left `next.config.ts` and costs static rendering of every page.
- [2026-11] The `ch_` session cookies are HMAC-signed and bound to the user and the cookie name; the secret is `SESSION_COOKIE_SECRET` and the check fails closed.
- [2026-11] Service-role writes must name an actor or a system job, so the audit log always says who or what acted.
- [2026-11] Owner decisions: MFA for staff (S-11) and a CAPTCHA (S-32) are deferred until the clinic has accounts to enrol; neither is built. S-13 was verified by the owner.
- [2026-11] Invitations: a person never chooses a token. The server makes 32 random bytes, emails the 43-character base64url form once and stores only its SHA-256. The link goes to `/invite`, which moves the token into an `HttpOnly` cookie and redirects, so it leaves the address bar, history and analytics; the page only reads, so a link scanner cannot use it up. Acceptance is one database transaction. Links last 3 days and work once. An existing account accepts by signing in with the same address. There is no second approval (PRD open question). The service role is used for the writes, with the person named in every call (`docs/SECURITY.md`, Documented decisions).
- [2026-11] Staff Google Calendar: each person connects their own Google account; no shared calendar and no patient booking, which stays in GoHighLevel. Only the `calendar.events` scope (plus `openid` and `email`) is asked for. Every Google call is made by the server, so the browser never holds a Google token and the Content-Security-Policy is unchanged. The refresh token is encrypted in the app (AES-256-GCM, key outside the database, user id bound in) so a database leak alone does not expose it; only the service role can read the column. Writes go through service-role-only functions that set `app.actor_id`, so the audit log names the staff member. A disconnect deletes the row rather than archiving it, because it is a credential, not a clinical record. Event changes themselves are not audited. Times are London time whatever the server's zone.
- [2026-11] Access by care team (clinic decision, 11 November): the rule lives in `private.in_scope()` alone, so one function change narrowed every patient-record policy. Leaving a care team sets `until` rather than deleting the row, so the history of who looked after a patient stays.
- [2026-11] Admins read every patient's clinical record, as the clinic decided (questions 2 and 27), reversing the earlier "admin is not clinical" design; they still hold no clinical write.
- [2026-11] Opening a patient's record must be logged: `/clinic/patients/[id]` refuses to render if `log_record_view` fails, because the clinic needs a complete log for CQC.
- [2026-11] Clinical notes are locked by a trigger that applies to every role, the service role and the table owner included, rather than by policies alone; a correction is a new row with the next version, and nothing is deleted.
- [2026-11] Education content is written by admins only; anyone who may open the patient's record assigns it. Patients reach courses only through `course_is_mine` (published, assigned, not archived).
- [2026-11, `fc7b5e8`] Lesson text is stored as a JSON document from Tiptap, not HTML. It is reduced to a fixed set of blocks and https or mailto links in the server before saving and again before rendering, and drawn as React elements, so no raw HTML ever reaches a patient and no HTML sanitiser is needed.
- [2026-11, `fc7b5e8`] Videos stay with YouTube, Vimeo or Loom and are embedded in the provider's player; `frame-src` names those three origins only. YouTube is embedded through `youtube-nocookie.com`. Any other link is offered as a plain link.
- [2026-11, `fc7b5e8`] Reordering goes through one definer function, `reorder_course`, which requires the full outline and refuses a stale one, so moves are all or nothing and cannot leave gaps or duplicates.
- [2026-11, `fc7b5e8`] Lessons, sections, files and covers are archived or replaced, never deleted; the `education` bucket has no update or delete policy.
- [2026-11] Wearable readings are typed in by the patient or an acting guardian until device APIs are connected; staff read them but cannot enter them.
- [2026-11] The doctor or admin invitation alert goes to a hard-coded address (`TEAM_INVITE_ALERT_TO`) from server code, after the invitation is saved, and never blocks it.
- [2026-11] Superseded in part: patient booking no longer stays in GoHighLevel (next items). The staff Calendar is still each person's own.
- [2026-11] A signed note is final (clinic decision, 12 November): no corrections at all, enforced in the version trigger so it binds the server too. A mistake is handled outside the note (Finding 86).
- [2026-11] Unmatched lab files are for PAs and admins only (clinic decision, 12 November), because a file with no patient could belong to anyone and doctors and nurses see only their care team's patients.
- [2026-11] Photos (question 23): patients see only their own; staff see the team's and their in-scope patients'. Done in the storage policy and by blanking `avatar_path` in the directory for non-staff.
- [2026-11] Removing a guardian switches off `can_view` and `can_act` rather than deleting the link, so the history stays; any PA or admin may do it, a patient may not (question 5).
- [2026-11] Booking (question 15) lives in Clinic Hub. The database is the arbiter: `book_appointment` re-checks hours, scope, care team and overlaps and locks the clinician's row, so two people cannot take the same slot. Google Calendar busy times are checked only when listing slots, because the database cannot call Google. Patients cannot cancel or move (48-hour policy); the team cancels. Calendar events carry the clinic reference, never the patient's name.
- [2026-11] Patient messages go through one function, `notifyPatient`, which honours each person's choices and never throws, so a failed message never undoes a booking or a release. GoHighLevel is used when configured so the clinic's team see the messages in the contact's conversation; WhatsApp is a GoHighLevel text diverted by the clinic's plug-in, and starts off.
- [2026-11, uncommitted, `fix/online-booking-confirmation`] Online bookings stay without a confirm link (the clinic's decision of 12 November 2026, as reported by the caller; Finding 205). Because a booking can be made in a patient's name without signing in, the patient is always emailed about it, overriding a saved email choice (`alwaysEmail`), and the email asks them to contact the clinic if they did not book it. When the confirmation reaches the patient by neither channel, admins and PAs get a second notice, because the notice `book_online` writes is created before any message is tried and cannot say whether it went.
- [2026-11] Call recording (question 16) replaces Fathom. The browser uploads the audio straight to private storage through a signed upload URL, so long calls do not pass through Vercel's body limit; only a one-hour signed link goes to AssemblyAI (EU region). The webhook trusts only the job id and fetches the transcript itself. The transcript becomes a draft in the recorder's name, which only they can sign. Patients never see recordings; nothing is deleted.
- [2026-11] Ask the clinic (knowledge base questions 14, 20 and 22): the conversation lives in Clinic Hub, not GoHighLevel, and replies are written only through a definer function that decides which side the writer is on and moves the status, so neither side can forge the other's message or skip the status. Emails carry no words of the question or reply: the clinic inbox gets a content-free alert, the patient a "the clinic has replied" notice with a link.
- [2026-11] Documents: staff upload through their own session under the existing Storage and table policies; the portal and the clinic open a file through a route handler that makes a 60-second signed URL through the viewer's session, so Storage decides again and the link never sits in a page. Staff opening is logged first (`log_record_view` with `documents`, since `09969cc`); the portal opens only the portal patient's shared documents. Sharing and unsharing is a flag, and removal is archiving; files are never deleted.
- [2026-11] Consent is append-only: each choice is a new `consents` row with the wording's version (`CONSENT_VERSION`), and the latest row per type is the current choice.
- [2026-11] Messages that leave Clinic Hub name no appointment type, document title or question text, since any of them may hint at a condition (S-124).
- [2026-11] Patient messages use `SITE_URL` alone (`getSiteUrl()`), so GoHighLevel works without Resend; staff are told what actually reached the patient rather than a promise.
- [2026-11] Rules first checked in the app are now also enforced in the database where it can: care-team roles, wearable ranges and dates, the recording upload path, a booking's location kind.
- [2026-11] Confirmations use the design system's dialog (`ConfirmButton`), not the browser's `confirm()`.
- [2026-11, `feat/small-items`] A leaver's Google token is revoked by a daily job, not by the trigger: the database cannot call Google or decrypt the token (the key lives on the server), so the trigger queues the encrypted token and the server revokes and clears it. A 400 from Google counts as revoked; a token is retried up to 10 times, then left for a person.
- [2026-11, `feat/small-items`] The portal patient is chosen by a plain `httpOnly` cookie, not a URL segment: every portal page keeps its address, and the cookie can only select among rows `get_my_patient_record()` already returns, so RLS stays the authorisation.
- [2026-11, `feat/small-items`] An allergy entered by mistake is archived (`archived_at`), not deleted, under the existing update policy.
- [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`] Stripe through its REST API with `fetch` and a signature check written here, not the `stripe` package: two calls do not justify a dependency. The API version is pinned in the request header.
- [2026-11] Stripe Checkout's hosted page takes the card, so card details never reach Clinic Hub; Stripe is told the amount and the request id only.
- [2026-11] Repeat requests are written only through definer functions, not insert and update policies, so nobody can mark their own request paid or approved, and a request and its medicines are written together after every line is checked.
- [2026-11] The payment is recorded only by the webhook with the service role, once (the unique event id in `webhook_events`, and `mark_repeat_request_paid` returning false the second time), and only for the exact fee. A mismatch is stored as a failed event and answered 200, as Stripe's retries cannot fix it.
- [2026-11] Online payment is optional: without the Stripe variables requests reach the team unpaid, so the feature works before the clinic adds its keys.
- [2026-11, `signaturerx`] SignatureRx over its REST API with `fetch` (OpenAPI 1.0.3) and `zod`, no SDK. The clinic holds one OAuth client (client credentials); each doctor has an integration code of their own, saved once and used for that doctor's prescriptions and status checks. Prescribing and signing stay in SignatureRx; Clinic Hub keeps the prescription's id, action and last known state only. No medicine, quantity or direction is stored, in line with the table comment.
- [2026-11, `signaturerx`] The signing PIN is typed into a form, passed once in the request body and dropped: no column, no log, no error text, not sent back. The form is off unless `SIGNATURERX_SIGNING_ENABLED` is exactly `true`, so the security review of that form can precede its use. `SIGNATURERX_BASE_URL` accepts only SignatureRx's own two hosts, so a wrong value cannot send prescriber details elsewhere. A typed integration code is saved only once SignatureRx has accepted the send, so a wrong one is not kept. The client uses `redirect: "error"`.
- [2026-11, `signaturerx`] The integration code table has no policy and no grant for `authenticated`: only the service role reads it, and a doctor saves their own through `set_my_signaturerx_code` (doctors only). The server asks `has_my_signaturerx_code` to know whether to ask for it.
- [2026-11, `signaturerx`] Writes to `signaturerx_prescriptions` are by the service-role functions only (`start`, `finish`, `fail`, `refresh`; no write policy), apart from the two [2026-11, `20261120120200`] functions that a signed-in admin or care-team doctor calls to settle a `sending` row (`close_signaturerx_send`, `confirm_signaturerx_send`: they check the row and the caller inside the function, and only a `sending` row can change), so a signed-in user cannot invent or alter a prescription reference. The send is opened (`sending` row) before anything is created at SignatureRx, so a send that fails half way is never invisible and a partial unique index stops a second one. A doctor's right to send is checked twice: by `can_send_to_signaturerx` in the action and again inside `start_signaturerx_send`.
- [2026-11] Admins read repeat requests but do not decide or withdraw them: admins write no clinical data (4.2), and flow 8 names doctors and PAs.
- [2026-11] The clinic's medicine list is seeded in the migration, not `seed.sql`, because it is the clinic's real list and must reach the hosted project.
- [2026-11] The run-out reminder stores the run-out day it warned about (`run_out_reminded_for`) rather than a flag, so a renewed supply is reminded about again.
- [2026-11] A weight-loss medicine is recognised by the first word of a list entry marked `weight_loss`, in the database and mirrored in the form, so current and typed-in medicines are caught as well.
- [2026-11, `feat/cqc-audit`] The results sign-off is a separate, permanent table written inside `release_report`, not columns on `lab_reports`: release and sign-off happen in one transaction, a report cannot be released without one, and a trigger refuses any later change even by the owner, which the CQC audit trail needs.
- [2026-11, `feat/cqc-audit`] Another doctor may sign off a report someone else ordered (the ordering doctor may be away); the database records `ordered_by` and `signed_by` instead of blocking, and the screens say so.
- [2026-11, `feat/cqc-audit`] Treatment options are columns on `clinical_notes`, so they fall under the same author-only draft rule and the same lock once signed.
- [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] Feedback is built in Clinic Hub, not GoHighLevel (reversing the knowledge base's earlier line of 12 November 2026), so answers and follow-ups stay with the patient's record and the CQC trail is in one place; GoHighLevel only carries the messages.
- [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] ~~Everyone who answers is offered the Google review link, whatever the score: asking only happy patients breaks Google's rules and the CMA's guidance on reviews.~~ **Superseded [2026-11, `38a918f`]:** the clinic decided on 12 November 2026 to offer the link after a green answer only, knowing that Google's review policy and the CMA's guidance discourage asking only satisfied patients (Finding 165).
- [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] The rating is a generated column computed from the answer in the database, so it cannot be set or changed by a client, and the same rule (in `submit_feedback`) sets the follow-up due date and the alerts.
- [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] Feedback is read by admins, PAs and the patient's doctors only, not by nurses or health coaches, because it may be about them.
- [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] Submitted forms are copied, as asked and answered in words, into one permanent table by database triggers and functions, rather than rebuilt from the source rows, so later changes to a request, question or the wording do not change the record. Permanence triggers refuse update, delete and truncate even for the owner.
- [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] Surveys are written to the database before they are sent, so a failed message is not sent twice; the run-out reminders and the surveys share one cron route to stay within the plan's number of cron jobs.
- [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`, `2171c59`] A person checks each quotable comment before it goes into the CQC evidence download: the patient agreed to be quoted without their name, but what they wrote may still name someone or say something about their health. Every download is written to the audit log first, and nothing is downloaded if it cannot be. Bank holidays are computed in the database (Easter by algorithm, substitute days by rule) rather than read from a table or an outside feed, so follow-up dates need no upkeep, at the cost of one-off holidays.
- [2026-11, `feat/clinic-diary`, `4f3c29c`] The diary shows every clinician's bookings with patient names to the whole team, as Semble does, because the clinic works from one shared diary (the clinic's default until it decides otherwise, `content/knowledge-base.md`); opening the record and moving a booking still follow `in_scope`. It is one definer function returning only the columns the diary needs, rather than widening the `appointments` and `patient_profiles` read policies, so the care-team rule for the record itself is unchanged. **Superseded [2026-11, `1309215`]:** the clinic decided on 12 November 2026 that admins and PAs see every clinician's diary and a doctor, nurse or health coach only their own. The rule sits in `clinic_diary` (so the database enforces it) and the page repeats it only to shape the filters.
- [2026-11, `1309215`] One person, one record: `book_online` matches an existing patient by email (contact or sign-in) **and** date of birth, because a shared family email is common and date of birth tells family members apart; the first name only breaks a tie (twins), and if it cannot the booking is refused rather than guessed. Nothing typed on the public form changes an existing record, because the visitor is not signed in.
- [2026-11, `feat/clinic-diary`] Online booking runs through the server's service role and three `service_role`-only functions, rather than granting anything to `anon`, so the database keeps "`anon` gets nothing" and every check sits in `book_online`, which repeats the server's checks. The server adds what the database cannot: the honeypot, the attempt limit by address and email, and the Google Calendar check.
- [2026-11, `feat/clinic-diary`] An online booking creates the patient record and the appointment in one transaction with no account: the patient is invited by the team after a person has checked the details, so nobody gets a portal account from an unauthenticated form. Adults only, because a child's record needs a guardian, which the public form cannot verify. [2026-11, `49cb8b8`] The booked clinician is put on the new patient's care team at once, so they can open the record and prepare; and until the record has an account, messages go through Resend only, so details typed by an unauthenticated visitor never create or change a GoHighLevel contact.
- [2026-11, `feat/clinic-diary`] Booking follow-up (calendar events and patient messages) moved to `lib/booking/follow-up.ts` so that staff booking, cancelling, moving and online booking share one path; a move removes the event from the old clinician's calendar before creating it in the new one.
- [2026-11] A separate `web-local` configuration on port 3130 rather than changing `.env.local`, so the local stack is available while `.env.local` stays on the hosted project.
- [2026-11, `feat/lab-webhook`] Labs push files to us rather than Clinic Hub pulling from them, with one address and one secret per provider (`LAB_WEBHOOK_SECRETS`). The request is authenticated by a bearer secret, not a session, so the route is outside the proxy's protected prefixes and the secret is compared as hashes in constant time. The webhook never reads values from the PDF: a PA still enters them and a doctor releases, so the release gate is untouched and the file is stored with `shared_with_patient` false.
- [2026-11, `feat/lab-webhook`] A patient is found only by exactly one match on email **and** date of birth (against `contact_email` or the sign-in email, unarchived). The name the lab sends is kept to help a human match and is never used to find anyone. Zero or several matches leave the file as `needs_match` under `unmatched/`. The webhook's answer never says whether a patient was found, and the lookup function is `service_role` only, so a lab cannot use either to learn who is a patient.
- [2026-11, `feat/lab-webhook`] A report is idempotent: the unique `(source_provider, source_report_id)` index and the `webhook_events` unique key make a retry return the same import. The report id is the lab's own or a hash of the file. The file is uploaded before the database call and deleted again if that call fails or finds a duplicate, because storage and Postgres cannot share a transaction.
- [2026-11, `feat/lab-webhook`] The service role is used by the webhook route (to look up, upload and record, through `lib/labs/receive.ts`) and by `matchLabFile` for the storage move only; the match itself runs through the PA's or admin's own session, and the function checks the role inside because the `authenticated` grant is wide. Request details are never logged. The upload limit is 10 MB in code, but the hosting platform refuses a body over about 4.5 MB first, so a lab with larger reports has to send a link (not built; see Finding 213).

## 9. Code conventions

- British English in code, comments, docs, UI copy and commits.
- TypeScript strict, no `any`.
- Tokens only: no raw colours, stray radii, shadows or gradients in JSX. Restyle in the component file with CVA variants.
- After every shadcn CLI run, confirm `cn` imports from `@/lib/utils` and no `cn` package appeared.
- One RLS policy per command, never `FOR ALL`, and one permissive policy per command per table; wrap `auth.uid()` and helper calls as `(select ...)`.
- Conventional Commits, one per logical change, on a branch.

## 10. Out of scope

[2026-11, `signaturerx`] Prescribing, signing and dispensing stay in SignatureRx. Clinic Hub creates the prescription there and keeps its reference and last known state; it does not store medicines, directions or the signing PIN, has no webhook or scheduled refresh from SignatureRx (a doctor or PA presses refresh). A PA sets a patient's date of birth, sex at birth, contact email, address, country and phone on the patient record. A send SignatureRx did not confirm is settled by hand, by an admin or a doctor on the team, after looking in SignatureRx.

Messaging, reminders, tasks, calendar, invoices and marketing (GoHighLevel); consultation notes and prescribing (Semble, for now). [2026-11] Clinical notes are now written in Clinic Hub (`clinical_notes`); there is no Semble import or sync of notes. Prescribing is not built (SignatureRx per `CLAUDE.md`). Wearable device APIs (Oura, Whoop, Garmin) are not connected; readings are typed in. Call recordings and transcripts are not built, though `note_source` has a `recording` value. [2026-11] "Calendar" here means patient booking and clinic availability; the staff member's own Google Calendar screen (`/clinic/calendar`) is built and in scope (Finding 67). **[2026-11, `feat/clinic-build-2`] Superseded:** call recording and transcription, patient booking with clinician hours, and patient email and WhatsApp messages through GoHighLevel are now built and in scope. Still out of scope or not built: patients cancelling or moving a booking, reminders before an appointment, syncing bookings with GoHighLevel or Semble, inbound GoHighLevel messages, tasks, invoices and marketing (GoHighLevel), SMS as a channel, and any import of Semble's notes or Fathom's past recordings. [2026-11, `feat/finalise-points`] Now built and in scope: Ask the clinic, letters and documents shared by the team, consent choices, and allergies, diagnoses and safeguarding alerts on the record. Still not built: patients uploading files from the portal (Upload results), prescribing and repeat requests, lab orders, adding or changing medicines, attachments on questions, matching an unmatched file from the UI. [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`] Now built: repeat prescription requests with the Stripe fee, the clinic's medicine list, and doctors adding, renewing, pausing and stopping medicines. Still not built: prescribing through SignatureRx (superseded: the connection is now built, see the first paragraph of this section), refunds (made in Stripe by hand), a GoHighLevel task per request, a PA requesting for a patient from Clinic Hub, and Stripe for lab orders.
[2026-11, `feat/cqc-audit`] `content/knowledge-base.md` now records that patient feedback (green, amber and red routing), Google review invitations and stored positive feedback are handled in GoHighLevel, not Clinic Hub (12 November 2026). Nothing in the code handles them. **Superseded [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`]:** the clinic reversed this the same day; feedback, its routing, the Google review link and positive feedback for the CQC are now built in Clinic Hub (sections 4.4, 4.7k and 6). Still out of scope: posting or reading Google reviews, any feedback in GoHighLevel, surveys after cancelled or no-show appointments, and copying forms other than repeat requests, questions and feedback (question replies are not copied).

[2026-11, `feat/clinic-diary`, `4f3c29c`] New patients now book on Clinic Hub's own `/book` page instead of GoHighLevel calendars, so "calendar (GoHighLevel)" above no longer covers new-patient booking. Not built: an online booker changing or cancelling their own booking (they contact the clinic), online booking for children, a deposit or payment at booking, matching an online booker to an existing patient record, an automatic portal invitation, reminders before an appointment, and moving a booking that came from another system.

## 11. Findings

1. [2026-11] The with-supabase template ships Tailwind v3; the brief expected v4. Upgraded to v4.
2. [2026-11] The shadcn CLI wrote `import { cn } from "cn"` and installed an unrelated npm package `cn`. Fixed to `@/lib/utils`; package removed.
3. [2026-11] The design-system README maps shadcn `--secondary` to surface-selected, but the tokens define `secondary` as bronze and the brief wants utilities named after tokens. Decision: the token wins; shadcn's role is `--secondary-role`.
4. [2026-11] The prototype sign-in uses a magic link; the brief says email and password. Brief wins for security and process.
5. [2026-11] The prototype sign-in panel uses a gradient; the design system and brief forbid gradients. Photography behind a flat `surface-inverse` overlay is used instead (the Imagery rule in the reference README).
6. [2026-11] The design-system README's generated index calls FilterChips a "square toggle", while the component README and brief say a rounded switch. Rounded switch used.
7. [2026-11] The Button README says six variants; the brief adds `link`, making seven.
8. [2026-11] The schema draft calls for a `staff_directory` view; the brief requires a `get_staff_directory()` function. Brief wins (security).
9. [2026-11] The template's `cacheComponents: true` was removed: all portal data is per-user and must not be cached.
10. [2026-11] `medications.start_date + supply_days` only models the first supply, so "days left" will be wrong after a repeat. The schema needs a last-supplied date. The patient home currently computes days left from `start_date`. **Partly addressed [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`]:** a doctor can renew a supply with `changeMedication`, which overwrites `start_date` and `supply_days`, so days left is right after a renewal; the first start date is lost, and nothing renews the supply when a request is sent to the pharmacy (Finding 125).
11. [2026-11] The prototype lists Eleanor in "Bloods at risk" with an appointment 20 days away, outside the 14-day window the brief and the prototype's own subtitle state. The app follows 14 days. By definition every at-risk appointment is already past its due date (appointment minus 14), so a "6 days left" card cannot occur.
12. [2026-11] `appointments` has no notes column, so the prototype's diary note line is replaced by the clinic reference.
13. [2026-11] The prototype diary has Sophie Marlow today and on a later date; the seed keeps both.
14. [2026-11] The prototype names staff such as "Dr Laura Bennett"; the seed uses those names. The health coach "Cecile" had no surname in the prototype, so the seed gives her the fictional surname Moreau.
15. [2026-11] `/portal` for a guardian of several patients shows the first only (own record first, then by name). A switcher is not built.

64. [2026-11] **Resolved for leavers [2026-11, `20261115120000`, `feat/small-items`]:** the trigger now queues the encrypted token in `google_revocations`, and `/api/cron/revoke-google` (daily, `vercel.json`) revokes it at Google and clears it, so the grant no longer waits for a manual step (once `CRON_SECRET` is set). Still open: a suspended account (`profiles.status`) with an active staff profile keeps its connection and nothing is queued (Finding 117). Earlier text: **Partly resolved [2026-11, `20261114120000`]:** trigger `staff_profiles_drop_leaver_connections` deletes the connection when a staff profile is switched off or archived (S-107). Still open: a suspended account (`profiles.status`) keeps its row; the grant stays at Google until the person or the clinic's Google admin removes it (`myaccount.google.com/permissions`). The migration comment said the app would revoke it; since `6164a90` and `09969cc` it says this correctly, so this is now a known manual step at offboarding, not an inconsistency. Earlier text: Offboarding does not remove a connection. The migration comment says nothing that can call Google is kept "once someone disconnects or leaves", but no trigger or job deletes the row when a staff profile is deactivated or archived or an account is suspended. The select policy then hides the row from the person, and the routes and actions refuse suspended people, but the encrypted token stays in the database and stays valid at Google until it is revoked. Also, `save_google_calendar_connection` checks `private.is_active_staff` but not suspension (the route does), and `getCurrentUser().isStaff` is based on roles, not on an active staff profile, so a deactivated but unsuspended staff member could reach the connect route; the save function would then refuse them.

Overnight events cannot be created here either.

When Google refuses a stored token while the page loads, the connection is deleted and the page redirects to `?error=expired`, whose message is "That connection attempt timed out. Please start again." That wording fits a stale state cookie, not a revoked or expired token. The server actions use a clearer message for the same case.

It is bound to the user id and compared with `state` in constant time, which is enough for its purpose; `docs/SECURITY.md` should confirm. The proxy's session-expiry path clears `ch_last_seen` and `ch_session_kind` only, not `ch_google_state` (it expires after 10 minutes and is path-limited to `/api/google`). `/api/google/*` is not under the proxy's protected prefixes, so the clinic's forced staff session limits by path do not apply there; the cookie-based kind still does.
