Auth, Storage and PostgREST behaviour (for example the storage policies in Finding 71) were therefore not exercised as the real stack would. `pnpm db:lint` and `pnpm db:advisors` have not been run on the five migrations. Not reviewed by `security-reviewer`: the biggest access change since the first schema (admins now read clinical records; coaches read unreleased results).
70. [2026-11] **Resolved [2026-11, `8399375` on `origin/dev`]:** `in_scope` and `inviter_covers_patient` compare with `private.london_today()`, and `care_team.since` defaults to London-time today. Not recorded here until now. Earlier text: `in_scope` compares `care_team.since` and `until` with the database's `current_date` (UTC on Supabase), while `addToCareTeam`, `removeFromCareTeam` and the list filters use London-time today (`todayInLondon`). During British Summer Time, between midnight and 01:00 London time, a person just added has no access for up to an hour, and a person removed keeps access until midnight UTC. The comment "access stops at once" is true only outside that hour.
71. [2026-11] **Resolved [2026-11] by `20261113120000`**, which adds nurses to the `documents` storage insert policy for in-scope patients (not covered by a storage test). Earlier text: Nurses may now enter results with a file (`enterReport`, `documents` table policies), but the `documents` **storage** insert policy (`20261102111728_storage.sql`) still names only doctors and PAs, and `enterReport` uploads through the person's own session. A nurse's upload is therefore refused and the entry fails with a generic error whenever a file is attached; entry without a file works. Not covered by a test (the stand-in has no Storage).
72. [2026-11] **Resolved [2026-11, on `origin/dev`]:** `log_record_view` now picks `actor_role` from doctor, nurse, PA, health coach and admin, in that order. Earlier text: `log_record_view` now logs admins, PAs and nurses, but it picks `actor_role` only from doctor, PA and health coach, so a nurse's or an admin's view row has an empty role and `/clinic/activity` shows no role for it. Everything else in the audit log uses the most specific role.
73. [2026-11] **Resolved [2026-11, `0539a05`]:** both comments now describe care-team access. Earlier text: Code comments now contradict the database: `app/clinic/results/page.tsx` ("a health coach reads released reports only") and `lib/data/clinic.ts` ("an admin sees no patients"). Since `20261112120100` an admin reads every patient and a coach on the care team reads unreleased results, so the clinic dashboard and results queue show them that data. The behaviour follows the clinic's decision; the comments are stale.
74. [2026-11] **Resolved in part [2026-11, `0539a05`]:** `CLAUDE.md` now says Clinic Hub holds clinical notes and Semble is retired in stages. Still true: no import of Semble notes. Earlier text: `CLAUDE.md` still says "Semble keeps consultation notes for now", and this document's product summary and section 10 said the same; the clinic decided on 11 November (knowledge base question 15) that notes move into Clinic Hub, and `clinical_notes` is built. `CLAUDE.md` should be updated by its owner. There is no import of existing Semble notes, so a patient's history is split between the two systems until Semble is retired.
75. [2026-11] Doctors and invitations: the database lets a doctor resend or cancel **any** patient invitation for a care-team patient (`inviter_covers_patient`), but the read policy shows a doctor only the invitations they sent, so the screen offers only those. A resend writes the resender as `invited_by`, so a doctor's invitation resent by a PA disappears from the doctor's list. The migration's comment ("A doctor can resend or cancel only invitations for their own patients") matches the database, not the screen.
76. [2026-11] **Resolved [2026-11]:** `8399375` sends the alert only when `VERCEL_ENV` is `production`; `2847f26` reads `TEAM_INVITE_ALERT_TO` from the environment with `drgina@theschoemanclinic.com` as the default. Still true: Dr Schoeman is alerted about invitations she sends herself. Earlier text: `TEAM_INVITE_ALERT_TO` is a real address (`drgina@theschoemanclinic.com`) hard-coded in `lib/invitations/constants.ts`, not a variable. Any environment with Resend configured, including local or preview builds testing the Team screen, emails Dr Gina Schoeman on every doctor or admin team invitation, including ones she sends herself. Consider an environment variable.
77. [2026-11] **Resolved in the database [2026-11, `20261114120000`]:** trigger `care_team_role_matches` refuses a relationship that does not match the person's `clinical_role`. Still open: the form still offers every relationship for every person, and `addToCareTeam` shows the 22023 refusal as the generic "We could not do that", not the database's message. Existing hosted rows are not checked. Earlier text: The care-team form lets any relationship be chosen for any staff member (a PA or coach can be added as `primary_doctor`); nothing in the database ties `care_relationship` to `staff_profiles.clinical_role`. Access is unaffected (it is by role), but the labels and the one-primary-doctor rule can be wrong. Admins are left out of the form's staff list; admins and PAs need no care-team row anyway.
78. [2026-11] A doctor on a care team can remove anyone from it, including the primary doctor or themselves (losing access at once), and add anyone, including another doctor as primary; the clinic answered that PAs, admins and doctors keep the care team up to date, with no further limit.
79. [2026-11] **Resolved in part [2026-11, `20261114120000`]:** patients and guardians now read only unarchived assignments of published, unarchived courses. Still open: no screen archives a whole course. Earlier text: `education_assignments` select lets a patient or guardian read their assignment rows even when archived or when the course is unpublished or archived (course id, assigning staff id, dates); the course itself stays hidden through `course_is_mine`. Harmless today, but wider than the comment "patients see their own" suggests. `education_courses.archived_at` has a grant and policy but no screen or action sets it, so a course cannot be retired from the UI, only unpublished.
80. [2026-11] Module video links are opened as plain links in a new tab; nothing embeds them, so the Content-Security-Policy is unchanged. Any `https://` address is accepted, not only known video hosts.
81. [2026-11] **Resolved in part [2026-11, `20261114120000`]:** trigger `wearable_readings_check` enforces the per-metric ranges and no future date in the database (S-116). The ranges now live in two places (the trigger and `lib/wearables/metrics.ts`) and must be changed together. Still true: staff cannot enter readings; the record shows 30 with no chart. Earlier text: Wearable ranges per metric (`lib/wearables/metrics.ts`) are enforced only in the server action; the table accepts any value between -1,000,000 and 1,000,000. A staff member cannot enter a reading on a patient's behalf. The patient record page shows the latest 30 readings with no chart or filter by metric.
82. [2026-11] **Partly superseded [2026-11]:** the page now also shows appointments, guardians (PAs and admins) and recordings, and `source = 'recording'` is set by `recording_transcribed`; `appointment_id` is still never set, so a transcript is not tied to its appointment. Earlier text: `/clinic/patients/[id]` shows notes, education and wearables only. Allergies, diagnoses, medications, results, documents and the record's other tables are not on it yet, although the clinic's decision gives the whole record to in-scope staff. `appointment_id` and `source = 'recording'` on `clinical_notes` are never set by the app.
83. [2026-11] The seed puts no nurse or health coach on any care team (tests rely on it), so locally the seeded nurse and coach see no patient. On the hosted project every doctor, nurse and coach must be added to care teams before `20261112120100` is applied (section 2), or they lose all access.
84. [2026-11] Finding 57 is partly addressed: `/clinic/patients/[id]` now logs a `patient_profiles` view (and refuses to render without it). Other screens that show patient data (the dashboard, results queue, invitations) still do not log a view.
85. [2026-11] `feat/clinic-build-2` state. The seven migrations `20261113120000` to `20261113120600` are **not on the hosted project**; the caller reports the six before them were applied there by hand, which this document has not checked. The only recorded test run is the first commit's "505 pgTAP checks pass locally (Supabase stand-in, not the CLI)"; no run is recorded for `13_ghl_and_booking` or `14_call_recordings`, nor `pnpm db:lint` or `pnpm db:advisors`, and `lib/database.types.ts` was edited by hand. Storage policies (the `recordings` bucket, the `unmatched/` and nurse changes, the photo rules) need the real Storage to be exercised. Not reviewed by `security-reviewer`: the first outbound messaging integration (GoHighLevel), the first third party to receive patient audio (AssemblyAI), the first public webhook guarded by a shared secret, patient-initiated writes to `appointments`, and the wider `Permissions-Policy`.
86. [2026-11] **Resolved in part [2026-11, `0539a05`]:** `CLAUDE.md` now says notes are final once signed. Earlier text: A signed note is final, with no correction path at all, so a mistake in a signed note can only be addressed by writing a new note. `CLAUDE.md`, item 20 of 4.14 and the earlier text of 4.4 and 4.8 described corrections as new versions; that is no longer true. Any correction chains already on the hosted project (from `20261112120300`, if a correction was ever written there) stay readable but the screen no longer shows "Corrected by". `CLAUDE.md` still says "Semble keeps consultation notes for now" (Finding 74).
87. [2026-11] **Resolved [2026-11, `20261114120000`]:** `register_import` accepts a file with no patient only from a PA or an admin. Earlier text: Unmatched files are PA and admin only in the table and storage policies, but `register_import` (replaced in `20261112120100`, not changed since) still accepts a doctor or nurse for a document with no patient. It is definer, so a doctor who knew an unmatched document's id could queue it, and would then be unable to see the import. Low risk; the function should follow the policies. Matching an unmatched file from the UI is still not built.
88. [2026-11] **Resolved in part [2026-11, `20261114120000`]:** a removed guardian can be invited again, and accepting turns the old row back on. Still open: neither the guardian nor the patient is told of a removal; `guardianAccessEmail` is still unwired; no screen shows removed links. Earlier text: Guardian removal: a removed guardian keeps their `patient_guardians` row with `can_view` and `can_act` false, and `create_invitation` refuses a guardian invitation when any link row exists for that person and patient ("already a guardian"), so **a removed guardian cannot be invited again**, and there is no screen to restore a link. Neither the guardian nor the patient is told; `guardianAccessEmail` is still unwired. The record page lists only links with `can_view`, so removed links are not shown.
89. [2026-11] **Resolved in part [2026-11, `0539a05`]:** `CLAUDE.md` now says booking is in Clinic Hub. Still open: no sync with GoHighLevel or Semble, so the double-booking risk below remains. Earlier text: Booking is in Clinic Hub, while `CLAUDE.md` still says GoHighLevel keeps the calendar (and Semble bookings), and `locations.ghl_calendar_id` is unused. Clinic Hub bookings are not sent to GoHighLevel or Semble and their bookings are not copied in, so the overlap check sees only Clinic Hub appointments and, for a clinician who connected Google, their timed Google events. A booking made in GoHighLevel or Semble that does not reach the clinician's Google Calendar can be double-booked. Which system is the source of truth for bookings is still listed as open in `content/knowledge-base.md`.
90. [2026-11] Conflicts with the reference and the client document: `docs/reference/schema-draft.md` has `appointments` written by "Server sync only"; the build lets patients, acting guardians and in-scope staff book. `content/knowledge-base.md` was updated for the decisions, but several older lines still contradict the build: "Appointments and payments are never entered by hand: they arrive from the booking system"; the results flow saying GoHighLevel keeps WhatsApp "so nothing here goes through it"; the appointments flow "Bookings live in GoHighLevel or Semble"; the integrations table (GoHighLevel "Planned", Fathom "to confirm") and its questions; and "WhatsApp and SMS join when the clinic's messaging system is connected" (SMS is not offered). For the knowledge-base owner.
91. [2026-11] **Changed [2026-11, `17b2f43`]:** `bookAppointment` re-checks the clinician's Google Calendar just before booking, so a clash created after the slot list loaded is caught; a Google failure still lets the booking through. The rest stands. Earlier text: Free slots read the **clinician's** Google Calendar during a patient's or another staff member's request: `freeSlots` decrypts the clinician's refresh token with the service role and lists their events (only busy times are used; titles are not shown). All-day events do not block slots. A Google failure is logged and ignored, so a slot may clash with the clinician's calendar; `book_appointment` cannot check Google. The screen offers 14 days, the database accepts up to 120. The booking's Google event is created only for a clinician who has connected; nothing tells them of a booking otherwise (no email, no `notifications` row).
92. [2026-11] **Resolved [2026-11, `29f602f` on `origin/dev`]:** patients and guardians read hours and busy times only of the clinicians on their own care team, and busy times only from now to 121 days ahead (`is_my_clinician`). Earlier text: `clinician_busy` and `clinician_availability` are readable by every signed-in person, patients and guardians included, for any clinician and any window: a patient can read every clinician's working hours and booked intervals (no patient detail). Intended for free slots; `security-reviewer` should confirm it is acceptable.
93. [2026-11] **Resolved in part [2026-11]:** `20261114120000` refuses a given location whose kind differs from the type's (a type with no location is still allowed, so a clinic appointment can have no place) and locks the clinician `for no key update`; `29f602f` caps patients at three open bookings. Still open: no description field on the type form; `appointments.type` copies the name; any in-scope staff member cancels; "Coming up" lists cancelled appointments; the book page logs no record view; the 1-hour and 120-day window is not tested. Earlier text: Booking details: `appointment_types.location_kind` is not enforced (a video type can take a location from the hours row, a clinic type can have none); the type form has no description field though the column exists; `appointments.type` copies the type's name at booking, so a later rename does not reach existing rows; `cancel_appointment` lets any in-scope staff member cancel, health coaches and admins included; the portal's "Coming up" lists cancelled appointments too; `/clinic/patients/[id]/book` does not log a record view; a type with `bookable_by_patients` false and the 1-hour and 120-day window are not covered by a test. No reminders before an appointment are built.
94. [2026-11] **Resolved [2026-11, `2847f26`]:** booking and results use `getSiteUrl()`, so GoHighLevel works without Resend, and `notifyPatient` writes a portal notice for bookings and cancellations unless the person turned portal notices off. Earlier text: `afterChange` (booking) and `tellPatientByEmail` (results) call `getEmailEnv()` for `SITE_URL`, which also requires `RESEND_API_KEY` and `EMAIL_FROM`. With GoHighLevel configured but Resend not, **no booking or result message goes out at all** (the error is caught and logged). Also, no in-portal `notifications` row is written for a booking or cancellation, so the "portal" choice for the appointments topic does nothing.
95. [2026-11] **Resolved in part [2026-11, `2847f26`]:** a suspended account returns `suspended` and the doctor is told "Their account is not active". Still open: a booking message for a patient with no account goes to `contact_email`, while a result message for one is skipped. Earlier text: `notifyPatient` returns `off` for a suspended account, and `tellPatientByEmail` reports that to the doctor as "They have switched results emails off". A booking message for a patient with no account goes to `contact_email` (and WhatsApp is off, as no choice can be saved), while a result message for a patient with no account is skipped: the two paths differ.
96. [2026-11] **Resolved in part [2026-11, `29f602f`]:** the contact is looked up afresh on every send with only the fields the chosen channels need, and a unique index stops two patients sharing one contact (that patient falls back to Resend). Still true: WhatsApp is a GoHighLevel text (SMS if the plug-in is off), and Resend sends are not logged. Earlier text: GoHighLevel: `upsertContact` matches an existing contact by email or phone, so a patient whose `contact_email` or phone is a parent's or partner's may be attached to that person's GoHighLevel contact, and the id is then stored on the patient for good (`set_patient_ghl_contact`; no screen changes it). WhatsApp is sent as a GoHighLevel text; if the clinic's plug-in is not active the patient gets an SMS instead. Only GoHighLevel attempts are written to `integration_sync_log`; Resend sends are not logged anywhere.
97. [2026-11] **Resolved in part [2026-11, `20261114120000`]:** a recording file can be written only at the exact path of the uploader's own `uploading` row (S-126). Still open: an `uploading` row is never cleaned up (the record now says "Upload did not finish; record again"); the bucket still accepts `audio/mpeg` and `audio/wav`; `checkRecording` still reads through any in-scope writer's session. Earlier text: Recordings: the `recordings` storage insert policy does not require a matching `call_recordings` row, so a doctor, nurse or coach in scope can put any audio file in a patient's folder. A recording whose browser upload or send step never finished stays `uploading` ("Not uploaded") for good: the screen offers no retry for that state and nothing cleans it up. The bucket accepts `audio/mpeg` and `audio/wav` but the action only webm, ogg and mp4. In-scope PAs and admins can read the audio (no screen plays it). `checkRecording` reads the row through the session, so any writer in scope could trigger collection of another clinician's transcript (the screen shows the button only to the recorder; the result is the same draft in the recorder's name).
98. [2026-11] **Resolved [2026-11, `2847f26`]:** a transcript cut to fit the note ends with a line saying so. Speaker labels are still "Speaker A", "Speaker B". Earlier text: `recording_transcribed` cuts the transcript body to 50,000 characters (`left(_body, 50000)`, the note's limit) without saying so; the prefix line takes some of that. A call of roughly an hour or more may lose its end silently. Speaker labels are "Speaker A", "Speaker B", not names.
99. [2026-11] **Resolved in part [2026-11, `29f602f`]:** the transcript and audio are deleted at AssemblyAI after collection or failure (best effort), and the audio link lasts 30 minutes. Still for the owner: the data processing agreement; nothing records consent per call. Earlier text: AssemblyAI keeps the transcript (and its copy of the audio) under its own retention: no code deletes a job after collection. A data processing agreement is noted as needed in `lib/env.ts` and `.env.example`; whether one exists, and the retention setting, are for the owner. The audio link given to AssemblyAI is valid for one hour. Consent to record is taken as given by the clinic; nothing in the app records consent per call.
100. [2026-11] **Resolved [2026-11, `29f602f`]:** the microphone and screen-share are allowed on `/clinic/patients/:id` only; every other page denies them. Earlier text: `Permissions-Policy` now allows `microphone` and `display-capture` for the site's own origin on **every** page, not only the patient record. Recording the call's own sound depends on `getDisplayMedia` sharing a browser tab with audio, which works in Chromium browsers; the recorder tells others to use Chrome or Edge when `MediaRecorder` cannot record. Not verified in Safari or Firefox.
101. [2026-11] **Still open [2026-11]:** not changed. Fathom is replaced by AssemblyAI, but `webhook_source` and `sync_system` still carry `fathom` and have no AssemblyAI value; the transcription webhook writes nothing to `webhook_events` and nothing to `integration_sync_log`, unlike the convention for other integrations.
102. [2026-11] **Resolved [2026-11, `2847f26`]:** the record page reads `?booked=told` or `not-told`, and `cancelAppointment` says what actually reached the patient. Earlier text: Staff booking redirects to `/clinic/patients/<id>?booked=1`, but that page does not read `booked`, so staff see no confirmation (the portal shows one). `cancelAppointment` always says "The patient has been told", whatever `notifyPatient` returned.
103. [2026-11] **Resolved [2026-11, `0539a05`]:** `CLAUDE.md` now names booking, recording with AssemblyAI, notes and GoHighLevel's role. Note: it says "GoHighLevel sends patient email and WhatsApp"; without GoHighLevel configured, Resend sends the email. Earlier text: `CLAUDE.md` is now out of step in three places on this branch: GoHighLevel "keeps messaging, reminders, tasks, calendar" (booking is in Clinic Hub; Clinic Hub sends some messages through GoHighLevel), "Semble keeps consultation notes", and no mention of AssemblyAI. For its owner.
104. [2026-11] **Resolved in part [2026-11, `2847f26`]:** the webhook answers 503 while the transcript is still working and 500 when collection throws, so AssemblyAI retries. Still open: `call_recordings.provider_job_id` has no index. Earlier text: The webhook route returns 200 for an unknown job and after a failed collection, so AssemblyAI does not retry; a transcript missed this way waits for "Check now" on the record. `call_recordings.provider_job_id` has no index; the webhook looks recordings up by it.
105. [2026-11] **Resolved [2026-11, `09969cc`]:** `20261114120100` replaces `log_record_view` to accept `documents` of that patient, and `16_questions_and_record` tests it; the route handler itself is still untested. Earlier text: **Staff cannot open any patient document.** `signedDocumentUrl(id, { logAs: "staff" })` calls `log_record_view` with `_entity = 'documents'`, but `log_record_view` (last replaced in `20261112120100`, not changed by either new migration) accepts only `patient_profiles` and `lab_reports` and raises 22023 for anything else. The helper then returns null, so `/clinic/documents/[id]` answers 404 "That document could not be opened." for every document with a patient, including the letters the team has just uploaded on the record page. Only unmatched files (no patient, PAs and admins) skip the log and open. No test covers it (`16_questions_and_record` tests the table policies, not the route or the log). Fixing it needs a migration that lets `log_record_view` take `documents`, checking the document belongs to the patient as it does for `lab_reports`; `activity_log` would also need to find the patient for that entity (it does through `metadata.patient_id`).
106. [2026-11] Question alerts: the content-free email to `QUESTIONS_ALERT_TO` (default `info@theschoemanclinic.com`) is sent from **every** environment with Resend configured, local and preview builds included, unlike the doctor or admin invitation alert, which `8399375` limited to `VERCEL_ENV = production`. It is sent only for a **new** question: when a patient or guardian writes back on an existing conversation (status back to `awaiting_reply`), nobody is emailed or given a portal notice, so a follow-up is seen only by someone who opens `/clinic/questions`. No staff `notifications` row is written for any question.
107. [2026-11] Who is told: `replyToQuestion` and the document actions call `notifyPatient(patient_id, …)`, which messages the **patient's** account (or `contact_email` and `contact_phone` when there is no account) and writes the portal notice only to the patient's own account. A guardian who asked the question, or who looks after a patient with an account, is not told of the reply or of a shared document. In the thread, a guardian's messages are labelled with the patient's name (`getQuestionThread`), since `author_id` is not used for the patient side.
108. [2026-11] **Resolved [2026-11, `09969cc`]:** `reply_to_question` returns whether the reply is from the clinic and the action uses that; the `from` field is gone (S-130). Earlier text: `replyToQuestion` decides whether to tell the patient from the form's `from` field, while the database decides which side the reply is from. A staff reply posted with `from=portal` tells nobody; a patient who posts `from=clinic` makes Clinic Hub send them "The clinic has replied" about their own message (limited by the `question` attempt limit). Low risk; the side could be read from the inserted reply (`from_clinic`) instead.
109. [2026-11] **Resolved in part [2026-11, `09969cc`]:** the update policy and grant on `questions` are gone, so status changes only through the two functions (S-133). Still open: health coaches, nurses and admins in scope can reply and close, which the draft gave to the team; the clinic has not decided on it. Earlier text: Two rules for question status: `reply_to_question` and `close_question` admit **any** staff member with the patient in scope (health coaches, nurses and admins included), while the older update policy "The team changes question status" (doctor, PA or admin in scope, columns `status` and `answered_at`) still stands, so a doctor, PA or admin can also set a status directly through the API without a reply (for example reopen a closed question, which no function allows). The draft's rule was the team in scope. Whether health coaches should answer patients' questions is a clinic decision; nothing records it.
110. [2026-11] **Still open after `09969cc`** (the portal document route was narrowed, the clinic questions queue was not). A team member who is also a patient sees **their own questions** in the clinic queue: the `questions` select policy is `my_patient_ids() or in_scope`, and `/clinic/questions` does not filter them out (the portal page does filter by patient). Opening one shows a reply form (the reply goes in from the patient side, since `in_scope` excludes their own record) and a "Close" button that the database refuses. Other clinic lists use `in_scope` alone and do not have this.
111. [2026-11] **Resolved in part [2026-11, `6164a90`]:** the patient record now shows each consent's current state, date and wording version, so the portal line is true. Still open: withdrawing (care included) sends no alert or notice to anyone, so a withdrawal is seen only when someone opens the record; nothing prompts a patient for a missing consent. Earlier text: Consent: the portal says a withdrawal is visible to "the clinic team … on your record", and the care consent says "the clinic will contact you" if withdrawn, but no clinic screen shows consents (the record page has no consent section), and withdrawing sends no alert or notice to anyone. `16_questions_and_record` shows a doctor can read them through the API only. `CONSENT_VERSION` is a constant to change by hand with the wording. No patient sees a consent prompt; a missing row reads "Not recorded" and nothing asks for it.
112. [2026-11] Conflict with `docs/reference/schema-draft.md`: the draft's `questions` table says each question "opens a conversation in GoHighLevel, where the team replies", and carries `ghl_conversation_id`. The build keeps the whole conversation in Clinic Hub (`question_replies`) as the clinic decided (knowledge base questions 14 and 22) and never sets `ghl_conversation_id`. Recorded in 4.14, item 29. GoHighLevel's team therefore do not see questions in the contact's conversation, unlike patient messages sent through `notifyPatient`.
113. [2026-11] The migration comment on `book_appointment` says the clinician lock "no longer blocks unrelated updates to staff profiles". `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` still conflicts with an ordinary update of a `staff_profiles` row (which takes the same lock); what it stops blocking is foreign-key checks (`FOR KEY SHARE`) from other transactions inserting rows that reference the clinician (care-team rows, notes, appointments for other patients). Two bookings for one clinician still serialise, which is what matters; the comment overstates the change.
114. [2026-11] Migrations edited in place after being applied by hand: section 2 records the caller's report that `20261111120000` and `20261112120000` to `20261112120400` were applied by hand to the hosted project, yet `8399375` and `a55db7b` later edited `20261112120000`, `20261112120100`, `20261112120200` and `20261112120300` (London dates, own-record exclusion from `in_scope`, `care_team_reject_self`, the care-team read policy, the `clinical_notes` insert grant and appointment check). If those files were applied before the edits, the hosted database lacks those changes, and `supabase db push` will not re-run them because the versions are already recorded. A human should compare the hosted functions and policies with the files, or ship the edits as a new migration. The same applies to `20261113…` if any of them reaches the hosted project before its later edits.
115. [2026-11] **Resolved [2026-11, `f235212`, `feat/small-items`]:** a portal patient switcher (`lib/portal/`, cookie `portal_patient`) appears when the person can see more than one patient, and every portal page (home, results, appointments, education, wearables, questions, documents, privacy) reads the chosen patient through `getPortalPatient()`. Earlier text: Every new portal page (questions, documents, privacy) works on one person, chosen by `getPortalPatientId()` (own record first, else the first person looked after). A guardian of two patients, or a patient who also looks after someone, cannot reach the second person's questions, documents or consents, as with Education, Wearables and Appointments. `/portal/questions/[id]` returns 404 for a question about anyone else.
116. [2026-11] `content/knowledge-base.md` and the reply copy changed on this branch (`6164a90`): Ask the clinic no longer promises a reply time and says the whole clinic team reads questions, which matches the policy (`in_scope`: PAs and admins every patient). The portal still uses the word "clinic team" for anyone in scope, health coaches included (Finding 109).
117. [2026-11, `feat/small-items`] `drop_leaver_connections` fires only on `staff_profiles.active` or `archived_at`. Suspending the account through `profiles.status` neither deletes the connection nor queues the token; the routes refuse a suspended person, but the token stays valid at Google.
118. [2026-11, `feat/small-items`] A token that fails 10 times stays in `google_revocations`, still encrypted, and is never tried again. Nothing alerts anyone and no screen lists it: a person must query the table. The `waiting` figure the route returns counts only the batch of up to 50, not the whole queue. If `CRON_SECRET` is never set, every leaver's token waits indefinitely.
119. [2026-11, `feat/small-items`] The `patient_allergies` select policy does not exclude archived rows, so a patient or guardian (and any staff member in scope) can still read an allergy entered by mistake through the API; only `getClinicalSummary` filters it. The update grant also lets a doctor, nurse or PA set `archived_at` back to null or to any time, and change `substance`, `reaction` or `severity` of an archived row; the app does neither.
120. [2026-11, `feat/small-items`] `google_revocations` has no audit trigger and the trigger's insert runs as system job `offboarding`, so the queue's own changes (revoked, attempts) are not in `audit_log`; the deletion of the connection is. `lib/database.types.ts` gained the new table and column with no `pnpm db:types` run recorded; regenerate it on the CLI stack.
121. [2026-11, `feat/small-items`] `CRON_SECRET` is read from `process.env` in the route, not through `lib/env.ts` and not listed in its schema, unlike the other secrets of the same kind (`TRANSCRIPTION_WEBHOOK_SECRET`). The Vercel Cron schedule assumes a Vercel project with crons enabled; the hosting plan is not recorded here.
122. [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`] Payment and decisions are not tied together. `decide_repeat_request` does not check `paid_at`, so with Stripe configured a request can be approved, sent to the pharmacy or declined before it is paid (the clinic screen only warns). `mark_repeat_request_paid` does not check the status, and a withdrawn request's Checkout Session is not expired, so a payment arriving after a decline or a withdrawal is recorded (a withdrawal is undone) and announced as "Repeat prescription paid". There is no refund flow; the knowledge base leaves refunds open.
123. [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`] `/api/stripe/webhook`: a paid session whose request id matches no request makes `mark_repeat_request_paid` return false, and the event is then marked `processed`, the same as a duplicate, with no alert, though the money was taken. A session with no payment intent stores an empty string in `stripe_payment_intent_id`.
124. [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`] Conflict: `CLAUDE.md` says GoHighLevel keeps tasks, the schema draft has `repeat_requests` read by a GoHighLevel task (`ghl_task_id`), and the earlier knowledge base had a task created per request. The build creates no task and `ghl_task_id` is unused; the team is told through Clinic Hub notifications and an email to `PRESCRIPTIONS_ALERT_TO`, and the updated knowledge base no longer mentions a task.
125. [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`] Sending a request to the pharmacy does not renew the medicine's supply. The reminder job skips a requested medicine only for 42 days after the request; after that, unless a doctor renews it with `changeMedication`, the old run-out day stands: already reminded, so no second reminder, while the home card and Prescriptions page show it as run out.
126. [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`] The run-out reminder matches open requests by `medication_id` only, so a medicine asked for from the clinic's list or typed in (same name) does not stop the reminder. The job also reads at most 5,000 medicines with no paging, and any beyond that are skipped silently.
127. [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`] A PA may request a repeat for a patient in the database (`request_repeat`), as the draft and knowledge base say, but no screen does it: `requestRepeat` needs a patient view and `/portal/prescriptions/new` needs the portal person to be able to act.
128. [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`] **Resolved [2026-11], committed before the merge into `dev`:** the committed migration let admins approve, decline, send and withdraw repeat requests, against 4.2 (admins write no clinical data) and the draft (status by PA and doctor). The working tree removes admins from `cancel_repeat_request`, `decide_repeat_request`, `decideRepeat` and the clinic screen, with a pgTAP check, and the knowledge base matches. The change edited `20261116120000` in place before it was pushed anywhere. PAs still approve a prescription request, as flow 8 says.
129. [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`] `announceRequest` (committed as `announcePaidRequest`) skips suspended admins and PAs but does not check the account status of care-team doctors, so a suspended doctor still receives the in-app notice.
130. [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`] `prescribable_medicines.sort_order` is seeded but unused: `getCatalogue` orders by name and no form sets it.
131. [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`] `PRESCRIPTIONS_ALERT_TO` is read from `process.env`, not through `lib/env.ts` (like `QUESTIONS_ALERT_TO`, Finding 106), and the paid alert is sent from every environment with Resend configured, unlike the team invitation alert, which is limited to `VERCEL_ENV=production`.
132. [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`] The Content-Security-Policy has `form-action 'self'` (`lib/csp.ts`), and `requestRepeat` and `payRepeat` redirect to `checkout.stripe.com`. With JavaScript, Next.js follows a server action's redirect in the client, outside `form-action`; a form submitted without JavaScript would follow a 303 to Stripe, which browsers that apply `form-action` to redirects block. Not tested here; worth a check in a real browser before go-live.
133. [2026-11, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`] This document has not verified that `pnpm db:reset`, `pnpm db:test` (with `17_repeat_prescriptions`), `pnpm db:lint` and `pnpm db:advisors` were run on the Supabase CLI stack for this branch, and `20261116120000` is not on the hosted project. **Update [2026-11]:** the user applied `20261116120000` and `20261116120100` by hand in the hosted SQL editor on 12 November 2026; the CLI-stack checks are still not recorded.
134. [2026-11, `feat/cqc-audit`] `lab_report_signoffs` is protected against update and delete by a row trigger, but has no `before truncate` statement trigger, unlike `audit_log` (`audit_log_reject_truncate`). `truncate` is revoked from `service_role` and never granted to `authenticated`, so only the table owner could truncate it, and a truncate would also skip the audit trigger. Low risk; recorded because the migration comment says "Nothing edits or removes a sign-off".
135. [2026-11, `feat/cqc-audit`] Reports released before `20261116120100_cqc_signoff_and_treatment_options.sql` have no sign-off and there is no backfill. The seed releases eight reports directly, so locally (and on the hosted project for any report already released) the report page shows "Released before sign-offs were recorded." The CQC trail therefore starts on the day the migration is applied.
136. [2026-11, `feat/cqc-audit`] The sign-off select policy is `private.in_scope(patient_id)`, so a **health coach** or **nurse** on the patient's care team reads the sign-off, including the doctor's action note, and PAs and admins read every one. The migration calls sign-offs "for the team only", which this meets; but it is wider than the doctors-only rule for safeguarding alerts, and item 7 of 4.14 (the coach reads no team-only tables) is already superseded. For `security-reviewer` to confirm.
137. [2026-11, `feat/cqc-audit`] `lab_report_signoffs.outcome` is `text` with a check (`action`, `no_action`), not an enum, although item 4 of 4.14 records the practice of using enums for fixed lists. `lib/database.types.ts` therefore types it as `string`, and `getReport` maps anything other than `action` to `no_action`.
138. [2026-11, `feat/cqc-audit`] `release_report` was replaced by dropping the two-argument version. Code deployed before this branch calls it with two arguments; once the migration is applied, that call reaches the new function with `_outcome` null and fails with 22023, so no report can be released until the new code is live. Deploy the code straight after the migration (section 2).
139. [2026-11, `feat/cqc-audit`] Nothing requires the treatment-option confirmations: a note can be signed with all four unticked and no options offered, and a `false` cannot be told apart from "not answered". Notes from `recording_transcribed` start with all four false. If the clinic means these to be mandatory for some notes, neither the database nor `signNote` enforces it.
140. [2026-11, `feat/cqc-audit`] `options_offered` is checked at 5,000 characters untrimmed in the database, while the action trims before checking; an all-whitespace value written straight through the API is stored as is (the action turns it into null). Minor.
141. [2026-11, `feat/cqc-audit`] **Resolved [2026-11, `1c56acb`]:** `dev`, with repeat prescriptions, is merged into this branch; `00_schema_checks` expects 48 tables and this document describes both features (42 migrations, pgTAP files 00 to 18). Earlier text: Branch interplay with `feat/repeat-prescriptions`: that branch has `20261116120000_repeat_prescriptions.sql` and `17_repeat_prescriptions.test.sql`, this one `20261116120100` and file 18. Each branch's `00_schema_checks` sets its own table count (46 here), so whichever merges second must update the count to include both, and the migration, table and test counts in section 4 must be combined.
142. [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] **No code marks an appointment `completed`**: no action, function or sync sets that status (only cancellation exists; the GoHighLevel and Semble syncs are planned). The committed `queue_feedback` surveyed only `completed` visits, so on the hosted project it would never send a survey. An **uncommitted** edit to the migration (and test 19) also counts `booked` or `confirmed` visits that have ended. With that rule a visit that was really a no-show, or cancelled in GoHighLevel or Semble but not in Clinic Hub, still gets a survey, and video appointments are included (the knowledge base lists that as an open question). The survey window is one to three London days after the **start** of the visit, not "the day after it is marked completed" as the knowledge base and `lib/feedback/send.ts` say, so a visit marked completed later than that is never surveyed. The migration edit must be committed and the tests rerun before the migration is pushed.
143. [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] `FEEDBACK_QUESTIONS.score` in `lib/feedback/constants.ts`, documented as "word for word as the database copies them", lacks the " (0 to 10)" that `submit_feedback` and the seed store in `form_submissions`. Minor, but the screen and the permanent record differ.
144. [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] The reminder goes to surveys with `sent_at <= now() - 3 days`. The cron runs once a day at about 09:00 UTC, and a survey's `sent_at` is a moment after the previous run's start, so whether the reminder goes on day 3 or day 4 depends on how early the later run starts. Low impact; the knowledge base says "three days later".
145. [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] `FEEDBACK_ALERT_TO` is read from `process.env`, not through `lib/env.ts`, and the amber or red alert is emailed from every environment with Resend configured (as Findings 106 and 131). A preview deployment on the hosted data would email the clinic inbox.
146. [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] `patient_feedback` has no permanence trigger, unlike `form_submissions` and `feedback_follow_ups`: an answered survey is protected by the revoked grants and the function checks only, so the table owner (or a future definer function) can change an answer or its rating. The permanent copy in `form_submissions` is the record that cannot change. `form_submissions.source_id` has no foreign key, and nothing checks that the source row belongs to the same patient; only the writing triggers and functions keep it right.
147. [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] **Resolved [2026-11, `2171c59`]:** `20261117120100` replaces `working_day_from` to skip bank holidays in England and Wales (4.7l); one-off holidays remain out (Finding 154). Originally: `private.working_day_from` skips weekends only. Follow-up due dates can fall on a bank holiday, and a red answer on a Saturday is due on Monday.
148. [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] `queue_feedback` accepts a patient with no usable account if a guardian who can act has one, but `sendFeedbackSurveys` sends through `notifyPatient`, which writes the portal notice only to the patient's own account and emails the patient's account or `contact_email`. For a patient looked after by a guardian with no account of their own, the survey goes to `contact_email` (or nowhere) and the guardian gets no portal notice; the guardian sees the home alert bar only. Not tested here.
149. [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] "Every form a patient submits is kept" covers three kinds only: repeat requests, the first message of a question (replies in `question_replies` are not copied) and feedback. A repeat request is copied once, when its medicines are written; the backfill copies existing requests with their current answers and skips any with no answer lines. Any new patient form must add its own copy step.
150. [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] `feedback_follow_ups` has no `created_at` (it has `followed_up_at`), against item 3 of 4.14 and the opening rule of 4.4, as `lab_report_signoffs` (`signed_at`) already does. Its `id` column exists only so the audit log names the feedback.
151. [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] **Still open after `2171c59` [2026-11]:** downloads are now audited (Finding 152), but opening the screens is still not logged. `/clinic/feedback` and `/clinic/feedback/[id]` show the patient's name, clinic reference and what they wrote, but do not call `log_record_view`, so opening them is not in the activity log, unlike the patient record and the report page.
152. [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] **Resolved [2026-11, `2171c59`]:** every download writes an audit row first (`log_feedback_export`) and is refused if it cannot; the evidence set holds only comments a team member has checked (`check_feedback_quote`); the 5,000 cap is gone (`listAllAnsweredFeedback`). The audit row records the set and row count, not which answers (Finding 155). Originally: `/clinic/feedback/export` downloads up to 5,000 answers (the full log with clinic references, comments and follow-up notes) and leaves no audit or activity row. The "evidence" set drops names and references but keeps free-text comments, which could still identify a patient; nothing in the export or the screens prompts anyone to check them before sharing.
153. [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`] Conflict between reference files and the build: the knowledge base's decision list first placed patient feedback, review invitations and stored positive feedback in GoHighLevel (12 November 2026), and `CLAUDE.md` says GoHighLevel keeps marketing; the same day the clinic reversed it and this branch builds feedback in Clinic Hub (the knowledge base now says so). `CLAUDE.md`'s summary of what Clinic Hub holds does not mention feedback or submitted forms. Recorded, not fixed (not this document's file).
154. [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`, `2171c59`] `private.is_bank_holiday` covers the regular England and Wales bank holidays only; one-off holidays (a coronation, a jubilee, a state funeral) are not counted, and Scotland and Northern Ireland's holidays are not either. A follow-up can fall due on such a day. Nothing in the code or migration says how a one-off holiday would be added.
155. [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`, `2171c59`] `log_feedback_export` is granted to `authenticated` and takes the set and row count from the caller, so any admin, PA or doctor can write `exported` audit rows with any count without downloading anything, and the row does not say which answers left. Rows are still attributed to the caller, so this cannot hide a real download, only add noise.
156. [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`, `2171c59`] `check_feedback_quote` allows checking any answered, quotable answer with a comment, whatever its rating, while the screen offers the check only for green answers and the evidence download includes only green ones. A checked amber or red answer has no effect, but the database and the screen state different rules.
157. [2026-11, `feat/patient-feedback`, `2171c59`] The "waiting to be checked" count on `/clinic/feedback` is worked out over `listAnsweredFeedback`'s 500 most recent answers, so older unchecked quotes are not counted, while the evidence download reads every answer.
158. [2026-11, `feat/education-builder`, `fc7b5e8`] `completeLesson` redirects to `/portal/education/<id>?lesson=<id>&saved=0` when the insert fails for any reason other than 23505, but neither the portal page nor `CoursePlayer` reads `saved`, so the patient sees the same lesson, still not ticked, with no message.
159. [2026-11, `feat/education-builder`, `fc7b5e8`] `moveItem` ignores the result of `reorder_course` (and returns silently on a parse or read failure), so a refused move (40001 when the outline changed in another tab, 42501, a network error) just reloads the unchanged outline with no message. `reorder_course` takes no row locks, so two admins moving at once are serialised only by the full-outline check.
160. [2026-11, `feat/education-builder`, `fc7b5e8`] `package.json` pins `@tiptap/react`, `@tiptap/pm` and `@tiptap/starter-kit` at exactly 3.10.7, but the starter kit's caret ranges resolve `@tiptap/core`, `@tiptap/extensions` and every `@tiptap/extension-*` at **3.33.1** in `pnpm-lock.yaml`, running over `@tiptap/pm` 3.10.7. The pin does not hold the editor to one version, and the mix of minor versions is untested upstream.
161. [2026-11, `feat/education-builder`, `fc7b5e8`] Limits disagree between layers: the `education` bucket and `education_files.size_bytes` allow 20 MiB, `uploadLessonFile` stops at 10 MB and covers at 5 MB; the bucket and the `cover_path` check allow WebP covers, `uploadCover` takes only JPEG or PNG. The server is the stricter layer, so nothing unsafe follows, but the documented limits differ by where you look.
162. [2026-11, `feat/education-builder`, `fc7b5e8`] On `/portal/education`, progress totals come from `listCourses().lessonIds`, which follows the reader's RLS: for a team member who is also a patient, draft lessons are counted (staff read drafts), so the card can read "2 of 3" when the patient can see only two. The player itself filters to published lessons.
163. [2026-11, `feat/education-builder`, `fc7b5e8`] `education_progress` has no update or delete grant and no `archived_at`, so a lesson marked complete by mistake stays complete for good, and no screen offers to undo it. It also has `completed_at` instead of `created_at`, and `education_files` has no `updated_at` although `archived_at` is updated, against item 3 of 4.14 and the opening rule of 4.4 (as Finding 150).
164. [2026-11, `feat/education-builder`, `fc7b5e8`] The `education` bucket's insert policy checks only the role and the path shape, not that the course or lesson in the path exists or is unarchived, and the `education_files` insert policy does not require the lesson to be unarchived. An admin can therefore leave orphan objects or attach a file to an archived lesson. Admin-only, so low risk.
165. [2026-11, `38a918f`] Conflict between the build and the reasoning recorded earlier: section 8 had recorded that offering the Google review link to everyone was required by Google's review policy and the CMA's guidance; the clinic decided on 12 November 2026 to offer it after a green answer only, knowingly. The code now follows the decision. Recorded so the compliance choice is visible; `docs/PRD.md` and `content/knowledge-base.md` hold the decision.
166. [2026-11, `feat/clinic-diary`, `4f3c29c`] Conflict with `CLAUDE.md` ("Screens read through the signed-in user's session (`lib/supabase/server.ts`), never the service role") and with the comment in `lib/supabase/admin.ts` ("Never use it to read data for a screen"): `/book` and `/book/details` read appointment types, clinicians, hours, locations and busy times through `createAdminClient()` (`lib/booking/online.ts`). There is no session on a public page and the reads return nothing about any patient, but the rule as written has no exception for public pages. Either the rule and the comment record the exception, or the reads move to functions callable by `anon`, which would in turn break "`anon` gets nothing".
167. **Resolved [2026-11, `1309215`]:** `book_online` now reuses an existing record matched by email and date of birth (4.7p); see Finding 200 for what that opens. Originally: [2026-11, `feat/clinic-diary`] `book_online` always inserts a **new** `patient_profiles` row: it never looks for an existing patient with the same email, name and date of birth. Test 22 books twice for the same email and gets two records. An existing patient who uses `/book`, or a new patient who books twice, gets duplicate records with different clinic references, and there is no merge tool. The two-open-bookings limit counts across those duplicates by `contact_email`, so it still holds. **Update [2026-11, `49cb8b8`]:** still open; the limit now also counts by phone number.
168. **Resolved [2026-11, `1309215`]:** `clinic_diary` now returns every booking only to admins and PAs, who already read every patient, and a clinician's own bookings to that clinician (4.7p). A clinician still sees the name of a patient booked with them whose care team they are not on (for example one booked by a PA through `book_appointment`), which this document records as the clinic's decision. Originally: [2026-11, `feat/clinic-diary`] Conflict with the care-team rule in `CLAUDE.md` ("access follows each patient's care team") and the draft's appointment read rule: `clinic_diary` gives every nurse and health coach (as well as doctors, PAs and admins) the name, clinic reference and appointment type of every patient with a booking in the range, care team or not, including archived patients. `content/knowledge-base.md` records it as the clinic's default "until the clinic decides otherwise". Diary reads are not written to `audit_log` (only record opens are), so who looked at the diary is not traceable. For `prd-keeper` and `security-reviewer`.
169. [2026-11, `feat/clinic-diary`] A patient made by an online booking has no care team, so the doctor, nurse or health coach they booked with sees the booking in the diary but `can_open` is false: they cannot open the record, move or cancel the booking, write a note or record the call until a PA or admin puts them on the care team. Nothing in the code adds it; the only prompt is the "New online booking" notice to admins and PAs, which asks them to check details and invite. **Resolved [2026-11, `49cb8b8`]:** `book_online` puts the booked clinician on the new patient's care team (from London today), so `can_open` is true for them. Anyone moved to later still needs adding by hand (Finding 170).
170. [2026-11, `feat/clinic-diary`] `moveAppointment` always passes the booking's **current** `location_id` to `move_appointment` and the form has no location field, so a move to a clinician (or day) whose hours are tied to another location is refused as "outside the clinician's hours". `move_appointment` sets `status` back to `booked`, so a `confirmed` booking loses its confirmation; it also does not require the new clinician to be on the patient's care team or the type to still be active, unlike `book_appointment` for patients. **Resolved in part [2026-11, `49cb8b8`]:** the location now comes from the new clinician's hours (checked against the type's kind), a `confirmed` booking stays confirmed, archived patients are refused, and `moveAppointment` checks the new clinician's Google Calendar. Still open: no care-team check for the new clinician (who may then be unable to open the record), and no active-type check.
171. [2026-11, `feat/clinic-diary`] The public `/book` page has no attempt limit on viewing. With "First available", each request runs, for every online clinician, three service-role queries and (if connected) a Google Calendar events read, so repeated anonymous requests can load the database and use up the clinic's Google API quota. Both Google checks (`slotsFor`, `stillFreeInGoogle`) fail open, so a Google outage can let a booking land on a time the clinician has blocked in Google. **Update [2026-11, `49cb8b8`]:** booking (not viewing) is now also capped at 20 an hour clinic-wide and can be guarded by Turnstile; viewing `/book` is still unlimited and the Google checks still fail open.
172. [2026-11, `feat/clinic-diary`] `book_online` locks only the clinician row, so two simultaneous requests with the same email for different clinicians can both pass the "two open online bookings" count and give three. The adult check uses `current_date` (the database's date, UTC on Supabase), not `private.london_today()`, so around midnight in summer someone turning 18 that day can be refused or allowed an hour early or late. Minor. **Resolved in part [2026-11, `49cb8b8`]:** the age check uses `private.london_today()`. The race on the per-email and per-phone count, and now on the clinic-wide hourly count, remains.
173. [2026-11, `feat/clinic-diary`] `bookOnline` ignores the `Delivery` returned by `afterChange`: if the confirmation email and WhatsApp both fail, the booker still sees "Thank you" and the team are not told the confirmation did not go (the admin and PA notice is the same either way). **Unchanged at `49cb8b8` [2026-11].** Since messages to these records now go through Resend only, a missing Resend set-up means no confirmation at all. **Resolved in part [2026-11, uncommitted, `fix/online-booking-confirmation`]:** `bookOnline` now reads the `Delivery` and, when neither channel says `sent`, `tellTeamPatientNotTold` gives admins and PAs a second notice. Still as before: the booker sees the same `/book/done` page either way.
174. [2026-11, `feat/clinic-diary`] `online_booking_clinicians` is not filtered by appointment type, so every clinician with any hours is offered for every online type (a health coach for a doctor's consultation, for example); the page then shows "No times are free" only when that clinician has no suitable hours. Whether each online type should be limited to certain clinicians or roles is a clinic decision (for `prd-keeper`).
175. [2026-11, `feat/clinic-diary`] The `/clinic/diary/[id]` page offers the move form for any `booked` or `confirmed` booking the reader can open, including bookings whose `source` is not `clinic_hub`; `move_appointment` refuses them with "This appointment was booked in another system. Move it there.", so the form is offered for a move that can never succeed. The diary also lists copied bookings from other sources alongside Clinic Hub ones with nothing to tell them apart. **Resolved [2026-11, `49cb8b8`]:** `clinic_diary` returns `source` and the move form is offered only for `clinic_hub` bookings.
176. **Partly resolved [2026-11, `e79f447`]:** `passesBotCheck` now skips the check unless both the secret and `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` are set, so a secret alone no longer refuses every booking; with only the site key the widget is still shown and never verified. Originally: [2026-11, `feat/clinic-diary`, `49cb8b8`] Turnstile depends on both variables being set together, and nothing checks that: with only `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` the widget is shown but never verified (bots pass); with only `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` every online booking is refused with the robot message, because no widget is rendered to produce a token; a secret outside 20 to 200 characters is silently treated as unset, so the check is skipped. The site key is read straight from `process.env`, not through `lib/env.ts`. A Cloudflare outage or a 5-second timeout also refuses every booking (the check fails closed, unlike the Google checks).
177. [2026-11, `signaturerx`] Conflict with the caller's brief: it said "25 migrations"; `supabase/migrations/` holds 43 files, 42 before `20261120120000_signaturerx.sql`, and this document counts 43. Tables are 50, as the brief said and as `00_schema_checks` now expects. 178. [2026-11, `signaturerx`] The migration's comment says the address is held where "only the clinical team reads". `patient_profiles` is also readable by the patient and by guardians who can view (their own record), so they read the address too. The new columns are in the insert and update grants for `authenticated`; who may write them is decided by the existing `patient_profiles` policies (not re-read in full for this entry), and a PA edits it on the patient record (Finding 179, resolved).
179. [2026-11, `signaturerx`] **Resolved.** A PA now sets the address, country and phone on `/clinic/patients/[id]` (`updatePatientContact`, `PatientContactForm`, `getPatientContact`). The doctor's message tells them to ask a PA. The country and phone are written to `patient_profiles.country` and `contact_phone`; for a patient with an account, `getPatientForSignatureRx` falls back to `profiles.phone` and `email` when `contact_phone` and `contact_email` are empty, so the form may show an empty phone while a send can still use the account's phone.
180. [2026-11, `signaturerx`] **Resolved, with a remainder.** The send is opened first and a partial unique index allows one open send for each request, so a second send cannot start while one is open or after one was accepted. What remains: if SignatureRx times out or answers unreadably, the row stays `sending` and blocks another send until someone checks SignatureRx and deals with it; **[Resolved, third pass]** an admin or a doctor on the patient's care team now settles it on the prescription page (`ResolveSend`, `resolveSignatureRxSend`, `close_signaturerx_send`, `confirm_signaturerx_send`): close it with what was found, or record the SignatureRx id, after which `checkSignatureRxStatus` can refresh it. Earlier text: there was no screen or function to close a `sending` row by hand, so it needed a database fix. See Findings 191 to 193.
181. [2026-11, `signaturerx`] The "draft" action also needs the signing PIN: `sendSchema` requires six digits for both actions, and the client comment says SignatureRx requires it either way. The PIN form therefore decides whether anything at all can be sent, and with `SIGNATURERX_SIGNING_ENABLED` unset, neither a draft nor an issue can be sent. Intended, but not the same as "drafts without a PIN".
182. [2026-11, `signaturerx`] The prescriber's address for the signature is read from `x-forwarded-for` (first value) or `x-real-ip` and must be IPv4, because SignatureRx wants one. Behind Vercel those headers are set by the platform, but this has not been tested hosted; an IPv6-only connection cannot send. Status of the doctor's code: it is held in plain text in `signaturerx_prescribers` (service role reads only, audit-logged on change). It is not encrypted, unlike the Google refresh token.
183. [2026-11, `signaturerx`] **Changed.** `start_signaturerx_send` now checks the sender through `staff_may_send_prescription` (doctor, active, on the care team today, not the patient or a guardian), so scope is enforced in the database as well as in the action. `_by` is still trusted from the caller because only the service role may call it. Both `status` and `detailed_status` are stored as null when SignatureRx sends none (`nullif(..., '')`), so `srxStatusLabel` shows "Not checked yet" for them.
184. [2026-11, `signaturerx`] `checkSignatureRxStatus` is open to doctors and PAs, but asks SignatureRx with the integration code of the doctor who sent the prescription, read with the service role (`createAdminClient`). That is a deliberate server-side use of the service role on a table no signed-in user may read; the PA never sees the code.

Earlier text in Findings 134 to 141 may describe the embed as a single object; the code is a list. Whether `lab_report_signoffs` is typed as an array in the regenerated file was not checked here.

187. [2026-11, `signaturerx`] The contact card (now also date of birth, sex and email) is shown to PAs only (`editsContact` is `user.roles.includes("pa")`), while `addToCareTeam` is open to doctors and admins too. An admin or doctor who finds an address missing cannot add it themselves. Matches the policy "PAs change patient profiles" (update is PA-only); not a defect, but worth the clinic knowing.
188. [2026-11, `signaturerx`] **Resolved, third pass.** `updatePatientContact` and the contact form now set sex at birth (female, male or unrecorded), so a PA can clear the "sex at birth" item that stops a send. Earlier text: no screen set it. A value recorded elsewhere in another form is not overwritten unless the PA saves the form (the select then shows it as an extra option and, if left, `F`/`M`/empty validation would refuse it: the PA must choose). `toSrxGender` maps the first letter to Male, Female or Other.

Harmless, but a doctor is not told the request was withdrawn in the meantime.
190. [2026-11, `20261120120100`] `private.request_repeat` was replaced whole by a second migration because the first is already applied; the function body now exists in two migrations. Anyone reading `20261116120000` alone sees the old `'{}'` initialisers (`20261120120300_reorder_course_lint.sql` does the same for `reorder_course`, Finding 198). The later file is the one in force. 191. [2026-11, `20261120120200`] The two functions that settle a send are open to any `authenticated` caller at the grant level; the gate is inside (admin, or `staff_may_send_prescription` for the row's patient). The page offers the forms to doctors and admins (`canResolve`), and the database narrows it to a doctor on the care team: a doctor outside it, or one who is the patient, sees the forms and is refused with the care-team message. A PA never sees them.
192. [2026-11, `20261120120200`] `confirm_signaturerx_send` trusts the id the person types: it checks only length (1 to 100) and uniqueness, not that the prescription exists in SignatureRx or belongs to this patient. A wrong id is found at the next `checkSignatureRxStatus` (an error), and the row has no way back to `sending` or to `failed` except a database fix. The confirm also leaves `status` null, so the screen shows "Not checked yet" until someone presses check. Audit entries for these two updates come from the table's trigger; whether it names the caller as actor was not re-read here.
193. [2026-11, `20261120120200`] The close reason is stored in `last_error` with a fixed prefix and is shown to anyone who can read the row (in-scope doctors, PAs, admins), as is any failed send's message. `lib/signaturerx/actions.ts` applies the `signaturerx` attempt limit per person to this action too (60 an hour per address, 20 per account), shared with sends and checks.
194. [2026-11, `signaturerx`] The contact form now writes `date_of_birth`, `sex` and `contact_email` on any save, so a PA who opens it and saves without a change writes the same values back, and the audit trail records an update. The form requires a date of birth; a patient record with a date of birth is always present (the column is not null), so this cannot fail for lack of one. The patient's `profiles.email` for a patient with an account is not changed by this form: `contact_email` is a separate column (Finding 179).

200. [2026-11, first numbered 177, `feat/diary-access`, `1309215`] Security, for `security-reviewer`: anyone who knows an existing patient's email address and date of birth can, from the signed-out `/book` page, book an appointment on that patient's record. Through `book_online`'s care-team insert this adds the chosen online clinician (any active doctor, nurse or health coach with hours) to the patient's care team with no end date, which gives that clinician the full record under `private.in_scope`. The patient's real contacts are then messaged through GoHighLevel (the record is not "unchecked", so `resendOnly` is false), while the person who typed gets nothing. Email and date of birth are not a secret between family members. The same applies to a clinician a booking is moved to (`e79f447`), though that needs a signed-in team member. Nothing in the code limits who is added, or tells the patient's existing care team. **Updated [2026-11, uncommitted, `fix/online-booking-confirmation`]:** the clinic decided on 12 November 2026 to keep this without a confirm link (Finding 205; `docs/SECURITY.md` recommends one). The mitigation is after the fact: the patient's own address is always emailed (even with appointment emails turned off) with a line asking them to contact the clinic if they did not book it, and admins and PAs are told when that message reached neither channel. The care-team grant still happens at booking, before the patient can object, and a suspended patient is not emailed.
201. [2026-11, first numbered 178, `1309215`] `book_online` skips archived records and matches only the email on the record (`contact_email` or the account email), so an archived patient, or one who types a different email from the one held, still gets a second record, as before. A guardian-held child cannot match (online booking is adults only), but an adult whose record has only a guardian's email and no `contact_email` cannot match either.
202. [2026-11, first numbered 179, `1309215`] On `/clinic/diary`, a non-admin, non-PA team member's own staff id is found in the `get_staff_directory` list filtered to doctor, nurse and health coach. A team member whose bookings exist but who is not listed there (for example a PA-only member mistakenly booked, which `book_appointment` should prevent) sees the empty state. The database returns their own bookings regardless; the page and the function apply the same role rule (`admin` or `pa`) separately, through `user.roles` and `private.has_role`.

209. [2026-11, `feat/lab-webhook`, uncommitted] **The pgTAP file was first numbered 24, like `24_diary_access_and_one_record`.** It is now `25_lab_webhook.test.sql` in the working tree, so the numbering holds; the caller's brief and the migration header still say "24_lab_webhook" only in the brief. 210. [2026-11, `feat/lab-webhook`, uncommitted] **Orphaned file risk in `matchLabFile`.** The storage move happens before the database call; if the database refuses, the file is moved back, but that second move's result is not checked, so a failure there leaves the document row pointing at the old path with the file at the new one. Also `receiveLabFile` removes a just-uploaded file on failure without checking the removal. Both would leave a file the database does not point to (or the reverse for the match case). Not seen in practice; for the code owner and `security-reviewer`.
211. [2026-11, `feat/lab-webhook`, uncommitted] **The route's comment and the code differ on the size limit.** `MAX_LAB_FILE_BYTES` is 10 MB, matching `lib/files.ts`, but its own comment says the hosting platform refuses over about 4.5 MB, so the 10 MB cap is unreachable on Vercel and the 413 reply says "Send a link to it instead" although no link route exists. The caller's list of changes says 10 MB cap; this document records both.
212. [2026-11, `feat/lab-webhook`, uncommitted] **The rate limit key is the provider, not the sender.** `allowAuthAttempt("lab-webhook", provider)` gives 600 an hour per provider, which stops a runaway lab but not a flood from one address of an unknown lab, because the secret is checked first and a wrong secret never reaches the limiter. Brute-forcing a secret is therefore not throttled by this action (a 32-character secret makes that impractical). For `security-reviewer`.
213. [2026-11, `feat/lab-webhook`, uncommitted] **No lab is agreed, so the field names are guesses.** `lib/labs/parse.ts` accepts many spellings of each field and either multipart or JSON, because the clinic has not named a provider or a format. Until a real lab posts a real file, matching by email and date of birth and the size limit are untested against it. Also no test file exists for `lib/labs/parse.ts` or the route; the only automated test is the pgTAP file for the database part.

[2026-11, `feat/lab-webhook`, uncommitted] **`match_import_to_patient` is granted to `authenticated` wide, with the role checked inside**, unlike functions elsewhere that rely on RLS-style helpers alone; it does not check that the caller can see the patient (care team scope), so a PA or admin can match to any unarchived patient. That is the clinic's intent for unmatched files (PAs and admins read them without scope, per `20261113120000`), but it is recorded here. Also `content/knowledge-base.md` is modified on this branch and not described by this document; the PRD keeper owns that account.
